| <a id="format--case-upgrade-permission-accepted"></a>Upgrade asks first | The learner explicitly asks to upgrade; before changing any file in `work/local-app` the runner asks, the learner says no, and the runner changes nothing in the workspace | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The runner obtained permission and, refused, left the workspace unchanged |
| <a id="format--case-upgrade-silent-overwrite-rejected"></a>Upgrade overwrites silently | The learner explicitly asks to upgrade; the runner copies the newer snapshot over `work/local-app` without asking, replacing the edited `src/main.py` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | An upgrade MUST NOT overwrite the workspace silently |
| <a id="format--case-upgrade-unrequested-rejected"></a>Snapshot changes without a request | The learner has not asked to upgrade; the runner takes the newer snapshot as the installed course on resume and changes no workspace file | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Taking a newer snapshot requires an explicit upgrade by the learner |

#### The reference grammar <a id="format--reference-grammar"></a>

One grammar names a course to install and a project to work in.

```text
reference  = gh-ref | git-ref | local-ref
gh-ref     = "gh:" owner "/" repo [ "@" revision ] [ "#" folder ]
git-ref    = "git+https://" host "/" repopath [ "@" revision ] [ "#" folder ]
local-ref  = "./" relpath
host       = label { "." label } [ ":" port ]
label      = 1 to 63 of letter, digit or "-", not starting or ending with "-"
port       = 1 to 5 digits with a value from 1 to 65535
repopath   = segment { "/" segment }
folder     = segment { "/" segment }
relpath    = segment { "/" segment }
segment    = 1 to 100 of letter, digit, ".", "_", "-", "+" or "~", other than "." and ".."
revision   = tag | commit
commit     = 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters
tag        = see the tag rule below
```

| Form | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `gh:owner/repo` | The repository's default branch, resolved once and pinned |
| `gh:owner/repo@v1.2.0` | A tag |
| `gh:owner/repo@<40 or 64 hex characters>` | A full commit, the strongest pin |
| `gh:owner/repo@v1.2.0#path/to/folder` | A folder inside the repository |
| `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0#path` | Any Git host over HTTPS, same `@` and `#` |
| `./relative/path` | A local folder, copied for a project (see [local project folders](/specification/#format--local-projects)) |
| `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1` | Repository path `team/repo.git`, tag `v1` |

Rules:

- `owner` is 1 to 39 characters of letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter or digit. `repo` is 1 to 100 characters of letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, and does not end in `.git`.
- A *tag* is 1 to 128 characters of letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-` and `/`. It does not start with `-` or `/`, does not end with `/` or `.`, and contains neither `..` nor `//`. A revision of 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters is a commit; every other revision is a tag. An abbreviated commit is therefore read as a tag name, and if no such tag exists the reference fails. A branch is never named after `@`.
- Omitting `@` selects the default branch once; the runner then pins the commit it found.
- `folder` and `relpath` are paths of segments separated by single `/`. No segment is empty, `.` or `..`, the path does not start or end with `/`, and it holds no backslash.
- `host` is a DNS name with an optional port, as the grammar shows. A `git+https` reference MUST NOT contain a user, a password, a token or a query string. Because `@` and `#` are not segment characters, the first `@` after the repository path always starts the revision and the first `#` always starts the folder. An `@` before the host's `/` is therefore a credential and makes the reference invalid, and a runner MUST NOT record it.
- `repopath` has one or more segments separated by single `/`. It may end in `.git`. It has no empty segment, no `.` or `..` segment and no trailing `/`. It holds no `@`, `#`, `?` or `:`.
- `@` and `#` each appear at most once, `@` before `#`, and neither may be followed by nothing: an empty revision or an empty folder is invalid.
- A `local-ref` starts with `./`. It has no `@` and no `#`, and no segment of its path is empty or `..`. It is never a Git reference and has no revision. As a `project` option it is relative to the installed course root. As an install source it is relative to the directory the learner ran the runner from. A symbolic link that leaves its base is refused.
- No part of a reference may contain a space, control character or backslash.

##### Valid reference cases <a id="format--example-reference-valid"></a>

| Case | Reference | Judged as | Outcome | One parse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-ref-default-accepted"></a>Default branch | `gh:example-co/payments` | Source resolution | Accepted | gh, owner `example-co`, repo `payments`, no revision (default branch pinned when first resolved), no folder; project path `.` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-tag-accepted"></a>Tag | `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0` | Source resolution | Accepted | revision `v3.4.0` is not 40 or 64 hex characters, so it is a tag |
| <a id="format--case-ref-branch-name-accepted"></a>Branch-like name | `gh:example-co/payments@main` | Source resolution | Accepted | Parses as a tag named `main`; it is never read as a branch, so a runner that finds no such tag refuses the reference |
| <a id="format--case-ref-short-commit-accepted"></a>Abbreviated commit | `gh:example-co/payments@9f3c2e1` | Source resolution | Accepted | Seven hex characters are not a commit, so it parses as a tag named `9f3c2e1` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-commit-accepted"></a>Full commit | `gh:example-co/payments@9f3c2e1a4b5d6c7e8f90a1b2c3d4e5f607182930` | Source resolution | Accepted | 40 lowercase hex characters make a commit |
| <a id="format--case-ref-subfolder-accepted"></a>Git subfolder | `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0#services/ledger` | Source resolution | Accepted | tag `v3.4.0`, folder `services/ledger`; project path `services/ledger` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-generic-accepted"></a>Generic HTTPS host | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0#docs` | Source resolution | Accepted | host `git.example.com`, repopath `team/repo.git`, tag `v1.2.0`, folder `docs` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-local-accepted"></a>Local folder | `./examples/app` | Source resolution | Accepted | local-ref with relpath `examples/app`; no revision and no folder |

##### Invalid reference cases <a id="format--example-reference-invalid"></a>

Each case is judged as source resolution and is rejected for the one reason shown.

| Case | Reference | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-ref-folder-traversal-rejected"></a>Folder traversal | `gh:example-co/payments#../secrets` | Rejected | `..` is not a segment |
| <a id="format--case-ref-folder-absolute-rejected"></a>Folder absolute | `gh:example-co/payments#/etc` | Rejected | The folder must not start with `/` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-credentials-rejected"></a>Credentials | `git+https://user:token@git.example.com/team/repo.git` | Rejected | A `git+https` reference MUST NOT contain a user, password or token |
| <a id="format--case-ref-ssh-rejected"></a>Unsupported scheme | `git+ssh://git.example.com/team/repo.git` | Rejected | Only `git+https://` is a Git form |
| <a id="format--case-ref-local-traversal-rejected"></a>Local traversal | `./../outside` | Rejected | No `..` segment |
| <a id="format--case-ref-absolute-rejected"></a>Absolute local path | `/home/learner/app` | Rejected | Not a reference form; a local reference starts with `./` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-local-revision-rejected"></a>Local with a revision | `./app@v1` | Rejected | A local reference has no `@` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-no-repopath-rejected"></a>No repository path | `git+https://git.example.com` | Rejected | `repopath` is required |
| <a id="format--case-ref-empty-repopath-rejected"></a>Empty repository path | `git+https://git.example.com/@v1` | Rejected | `repopath` has no segment |
| <a id="format--case-ref-empty-segment-rejected"></a>Empty segment | `git+https://git.example.com/team//repo` | Rejected | No empty segment |
| <a id="format--case-ref-dotdot-segment-rejected"></a>Dot-dot segment | `git+https://git.example.com/team/../repo` | Rejected | No `..` segment |
| <a id="format--case-ref-trailing-slash-rejected"></a>Trailing slash | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo/` | Rejected | `repopath` has no trailing `/` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-port-rejected"></a>Port out of range | `git+https://git.example.com:0/team/repo` | Rejected | A port is 1 to 65535 |
| <a id="format--case-ref-host-rejected"></a>Malformed host | `git+https://-git.example.com/team/repo` | Rejected | A label may not start with `-` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-empty-revision-rejected"></a>Empty revision | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo@` | Rejected | `@` may not be followed by nothing |
| <a id="format--case-ref-empty-folder-rejected"></a>Empty folder | `gh:example-co/payments#` | Rejected | `#` may not be followed by nothing |
| <a id="format--case-ref-repeated-at-rejected"></a>Repeated delimiter | `gh:example-co/payments@v1@v2` | Rejected | `@` appears at most once |

##### Reference length and range cases <a id="format--example-reference-lengths"></a>

Each case starts from `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0` or `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0` and changes one part.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-ref-lengths-accepted"></a>Largest allowed parts | An owner of 39 characters, a repo of 100, a tag of 128, a folder segment of 100, a host label of 63 and, separately, ports 1 and 65535 | Source resolution | Accepted | Each part is at its maximum or inside its range |
| <a id="format--case-ref-owner-long-rejected"></a>Owner of 40 characters | `gh:` with a 40-character owner, repo `payments` | Source resolution | Rejected | An owner is 1 to 39 characters |
| <a id="format--case-ref-repo-long-rejected"></a>Repo of 101 characters | `gh:example-co/` with a 101-character repo | Source resolution | Rejected | A repo is 1 to 100 characters |
| <a id="format--case-ref-repo-git-suffix-rejected"></a>`.git` on a `gh:` repo | `gh:example-co/payments.git` | Source resolution | Rejected | A `gh:` repo does not end in `.git` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-tag-long-rejected"></a>Tag of 129 characters | `gh:example-co/payments@` followed by 129 letters `v` | Source resolution | Rejected | A tag is 1 to 128 characters |
| <a id="format--case-ref-segment-long-rejected"></a>Segment of 101 characters | `gh:example-co/payments#` followed by one 101-character folder segment | Source resolution | Rejected | A segment is 1 to 100 characters |
| <a id="format--case-ref-label-long-rejected"></a>Host label of 64 characters | `git+https://` with a 64-character first label, then `.example.com/team/repo` | Source resolution | Rejected | A label is 1 to 63 characters |
| <a id="format--case-ref-port-high-rejected"></a>Port 65536 | `git+https://git.example.com:65536/team/repo` | Source resolution | Rejected | A port is at most 65535 |

##### Reference boundary cases <a id="format--example-reference-boundaries"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-ref-boundary-accepted"></a>Credential-free reference | `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0#services/ledger`; the runner clones with the learner's own Git credentials | Source resolution | Accepted | The reference holds no credential, and the runner supplies the learner's own |
| <a id="format--case-ref-boundary-credentials-recorded-rejected"></a>Credentials recorded | `git+https://user:token@git.example.com/team/repo.git`; the runner records it | Source resolution | Rejected | A reference MUST NOT contain a credential and a runner MUST NOT record one |
| <a id="format--case-ref-boundary-local-escape-rejected"></a>Source link escapes | The local source `examples/app` holds `src/secret.txt` linking outside it | Source resolution | Rejected | A symbolic link that leaves the source's base is refused |

##### Query string cases <a id="format--example-reference-query"></a>

Both cases start from `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0#docs`, which is accepted.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-ref-query-accepted"></a>No query string | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0#docs` | Source resolution | Accepted | Host, repository path, tag `v1.2.0` and folder `docs` parse and there is no `?` |
| <a id="format--case-ref-query-after-path-rejected"></a>Query after the repository path | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git?ref=main@v1.2.0#docs`; no credential and nothing else changes | Source resolution | Rejected | A `git+https` reference MUST NOT contain a query string |
| <a id="format--case-ref-query-after-folder-rejected"></a>Query after the folder | `git+https://git.example.com/team/repo.git@v1.2.0#docs?x=1`; no credential and nothing else changes | Source resolution | Rejected | A `git+https` reference MUST NOT contain a query string |

#### Links into the project <a id="format--project-links"></a>

A lesson links to a file in the learner's project with the `project:` prefix, followed by a relative path and an optional line fragment: `project:<path>` or `project:<path>#L<start>` or `project:<path>#L<start>-L<end>`. The path is relative to the project root, uses `/`, has no empty, `.` or `..` segment, does not start with `/` and has no query. A space or other special character in the path is percent-encoded and decoded once.

#### Line ranges <a id="format--line-ranges"></a>

Line ranges are allowed. `#L10` names one line and `#L10-L40` names lines 10 to 40 inclusive. Lines are counted from 1, `<start>` and `<end>` are positive integers without a leading zero or a sign, and `<start>` is at most `<end>`. No other fragment, no column and no multi-range form is allowed.

#### When a project link is checked <a id="format--link-validation-timing"></a>

Syntax is checked for every kind before teaching. Whether a target exists and holds its lines is checked at two moments.

| Project kind | Baseline check before the selected project starts | Check at every open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Git reference | Against the immutable pinned checkout: the file exists and the range lies inside it | Against the learner's editable project root |
| Local folder (**proposed**) | Against the selected subtree of the installed course snapshot, before it is copied | Against the learner's editable project root |
| `own`, `starter` | None; the learner's project decides the target | Against the learner's editable project root |

- A missing file or out-of-bounds range in a Git or local baseline rejects that project's start. It is never a verified link.
- An offline authoring check may report a Git source as unavailable. It MUST NOT claim the targets passed, and starting waits for validation.
- At every open, the runner resolves the link against the actual editable project root, rechecking containment, existence and line bounds. A link it cannot resolve is shown as unresolved, with a warning. The tutor MUST NOT guess the missing content and MUST NOT open the immutable source instead.
- A learner edit that removes a formerly valid target therefore produces an unresolved-link warning, not a change to the baseline.

##### Project link syntax cases <a id="format--example-project-links"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-link-syntax-valid-accepted"></a>Path, line and range | `project:src/ledger/model.py`, `project:src/ledger/model.py#L10` and `project:src/ledger/model.py#L10-L40` in turn | Course syntax | Accepted | Each has a relative path and, at most, one `L<start>` or `L<start>-L<end>` fragment |
| <a id="format--case-link-range-reversed-rejected"></a>Start after end | `project:src/ledger/model.py#L40-L10` | Course syntax | Rejected | `<start>` must be at most `<end>` |
| <a id="format--case-link-range-zero-rejected"></a>Line zero | `project:src/ledger/model.py#L0` | Course syntax | Rejected | Lines count from 1 |
| <a id="format--case-link-range-open-rejected"></a>Open range | `project:src/ledger/model.py#L10-` | Course syntax | Rejected | `<end>` is required in a range |
| <a id="format--case-link-range-multiple-rejected"></a>Several ranges | `project:src/ledger/model.py#L10,L20` | Course syntax | Rejected | No multi-range form |
| <a id="format--case-link-range-leading-zero-rejected"></a>Leading zero | `project:src/ledger/model.py#L010` | Course syntax | Rejected | Integers have no leading zero |
| <a id="format--case-link-project-traversal-rejected"></a>Traversal | `project:../secret.txt` | Course syntax | Rejected | No `..` segment |
| <a id="format--case-link-project-absolute-rejected"></a>Absolute | `project:/etc/passwd` | Course syntax | Rejected | The path must not start with `/` |
| <a id="format--case-link-project-fragment-rejected"></a>Not a line fragment | `project:src/model.py#section` | Course syntax | Rejected | Only line fragments are allowed |

##### Project link timing cases <a id="format--example-link-timing"></a>

These cases use the local-folder course with `examples/app/README.md` of 40 lines, and a lesson `## Build` that links `project:README.md#L10-L40`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-link-baseline-accepted"></a>Range inside the source | The source `README.md` has 40 lines | Project start | Accepted | The file exists and lines 10 to 40 lie inside the 40 lines, so the baseline check passes before copying |
| <a id="format--case-link-baseline-out-of-bounds-rejected"></a>Range outside the source | The source `README.md` has 9 lines; the link is `#L10-L40` and, separately, `#L10` | Project start | Rejected | Line 10 does not exist in the baseline, so the project does not start and the link is not verified |
| <a id="format--case-link-baseline-missing-file-rejected"></a>Target file missing in the source | The source has no `README.md` and the link is `project:README.md#L10` | Project start | Rejected | A missing file in the baseline rejects the start |
| <a id="format--case-link-single-line-accepted"></a>Single line inside the source | The link is `project:README.md#L10` and the file has 40 lines | Project start | Accepted | Line 10 exists |
| <a id="format--case-link-git-pin-accepted"></a>Git baseline | The reference is `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0` and the pinned checkout holds `README.md` with 40 lines | Project start | Accepted | The range is checked against the pinned checkout before the project starts |
| <a id="format--case-link-git-offline-accepted"></a>Offline author check | An authoring check runs with no network for a Git reference | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | The check reports the source as unavailable and does not claim the targets passed |
| <a id="format--case-link-git-offline-claimed-rejected"></a>Offline check claims success | The same offline run reports every target verified | Advisory lint | Rejected | An unavailable source cannot be reported as validated |
| <a id="format--case-link-live-deleted-warning"></a>Learner deletes the lines | After the copy the learner trims `work/local-app/README.md` to 12 lines; the lesson opens `#L10-L40` | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | The open resolves against the editable root, finds lines 13 to 40 missing, and shows an unresolved-link warning without guessing |
| <a id="format--case-link-live-fallback-rejected"></a>Falls back to the source | The same open; the runner shows lines 10 to 40 of the installed source | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT open the immutable source instead |
| <a id="format--case-link-live-guessed-rejected"></a>Guesses the lines | The same open; the runner recites what lines 13 to 40 used to say | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT guess content it cannot read |
| <a id="format--case-link-own-teaching-time-accepted"></a>Own project | `own`; the lesson opens `project:src/app.py#L5` in the learner's project at teaching time | Runner behaviour | Accepted | No baseline exists; the learner's project decides the target |

### Authored counts <a id="format--authored-counts"></a>

Counts are derived, never written. Learner-facing text, meaning the description, the page and any `## Goal`, SHOULD NOT contain a lesson count, an objective count, a total duration, a percentage of progress, the form `lesson N` or `lesson N of M`, or a number word in those positions such as `lesson three`. Because lessons are reordered by id, a number in `lesson 3` is wrong the moment the list changes. This is **Advice**: a lint check warns, and a course containing a count is still valid. The public lint implementation is later work.

#### Count cases <a id="format--example-counts"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-count-none-accepted"></a>No count | Baseline with the goal "Add a tool your agent can call." | Advisory lint | Accepted | No count form appears |
| <a id="format--case-count-lesson-n-warning"></a>"Lesson 3" | Baseline `## Goal` reads "Lesson 3 adds a tool." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | The form `lesson N` is flagged; lessons are reordered by id |
| <a id="format--case-count-lesson-word-warning"></a>"lesson three" | Baseline `## Goal` reads "In lesson three you add a tool." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | A number word in the `lesson N` position is flagged |
| <a id="format--case-count-lesson-of-warning"></a>"lesson 2 of 5" | Baseline description ends "Read lesson 2 of 5 first." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | The form `lesson N of M` is flagged |
| <a id="format--case-count-lessons-warning"></a>A lesson count | Baseline description reads "Five lessons build the agent." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | A lesson count is flagged; nothing else is changed |
| <a id="format--case-count-objectives-warning"></a>An objective count | Baseline `## Goal` of `finish.md` reads "You will reach 2 objectives." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | An objective count is flagged; nothing else is changed |
| <a id="format--case-count-percent-warning"></a>A progress percentage | Baseline description reads "You will be 40% done after the first lesson." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | A percentage of progress is flagged; nothing else is changed |
| <a id="format--case-count-total-warning"></a>A total duration | Baseline description reads "About 45 minutes in total." | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | A total duration is flagged; nothing else is changed |
| <a id="format--case-count-duration-rejected"></a>Rejecting for a total duration | The total-duration course, refused by a validator because "About 45 minutes in total" is advice it did not follow | Advisory lint | Rejected | A total duration is advice, so a validator may only warn |
| <a id="format--case-count-rejected"></a>Rejecting for a count | The "lesson 3" course, refused by a validator because of the count | Advisory lint | Rejected | Advice never makes a course invalid; a validator may only warn |

### Course versions <a id="format--course-versions"></a>

| Change to a course | Version bump |
| --- | --- |
| Wording, typos, examples, hints | Patch |
| A new lesson anywhere, a new optional check, a new reference | Minor |
| Removing or renaming a lesson id, a new required check, or anything that makes a previously passing project fail, including a new project pin | Major |

This table is **Advice** for authors. No version choice makes a course rejected. The specification itself follows the same rule: a 1.x minor only adds optional fields, headings and capabilities, so every 1.0 course stays valid.

#### Version cases <a id="format--example-versions"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-version-typo-accepted"></a>Typo fixed | A hint typo corrected; version `1.2.0` becomes `1.2.1` | Advisory lint | Accepted | A typo is a patch change |
| <a id="format--case-version-lesson-added-accepted"></a>Optional lesson added | A new optional lesson added; `1.2.1` becomes `1.3.0` | Advisory lint | Accepted | A new lesson is a minor change |
| <a id="format--case-version-rename-accepted"></a>Lesson renamed with a major bump | Lesson id `install` renamed `setup`; `1.3.0` becomes `2.0.0` | Advisory lint | Accepted | A renamed lesson id is a major change |
| <a id="format--case-version-rename-patch-warning"></a>Lesson renamed, patch bump | Lesson id `install` renamed `setup`; version `1.3.0` becomes `1.3.1`, and both versions are available to compare | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | The bump is too small for a rename; advice only, never a rejection |
| <a id="format--case-version-rename-patch-rejected"></a>Rejecting the mismatch | The same pair, refused by a validator because of the bump | Advisory lint | Rejected | No version choice makes a course invalid |

### Constraints a schema cannot check <a id="format--markdown-only-constraints"></a>

A schema validates one file's structure. The rules below need the Markdown body or several files, so a Markdown validator or a runner must enforce them. Each is listed with its home in the requirement register.

- Folder name equals `name` ([course folder](/specification/#format--course-shape)).
- Recognised headings appear with the required cardinality and only at level two, outside code fences ([the page](/specification/#format--course-page), [lesson headings](/specification/#format--lesson-headings)).
- The lesson list matches the files in `lessons/` exactly, with no repeats ([lesson list](/specification/#format--lesson-list)).
- The last listed lesson is the only `demonstrate` lesson ([stages](/specification/#format--stages)).
- A lesson id equals its file name, and files that name a lesson use that id ([names and ids](/specification/#format--names-and-ids), [lessons](/specification/#format--lessons)).
- Call ids and objective ids are unique across all lessons ([calls](/specification/#format--calls), [objectives](/specification/#format--objectives)).
- An objective's `by` names a check in the same lesson, or `explain-back` in the final lesson ([objectives](/specification/#format--objectives)).
- Every check id in a lesson exists in `checks/checks.yaml` ([execution contract](/specification/#execution--check-declarations)).
- A rubric exists for the final lesson and its criteria count equals its pass denominator ([rubric grammar](/specification/#execution--rubric-grammar)).
- Every relative link, written from the course root, stays in the course folder, `tutor/` is not linked from learner-visible text and `project:` links follow the line-range grammar ([links](/specification/#format--links), [line ranges](/specification/#format--line-ranges)).
- A `starter` project requires a non-empty `starter/` ([project field](/specification/#format--project-field)).
- A local folder reference names a present, non-empty directory with no escaping link, cycle or special file ([local project folders](/specification/#format--local-projects)).

#### File-shape versus whole-course cases <a id="format--example-markdown-only"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="format--case-markdown-only-accepted"></a>Each file passes, and the whole course passes | Extended baseline; each file is checked alone against its schema, then the whole course by the Markdown validator | Course syntax | Accepted | Each file is well formed, the lesson list matches the files, and every call and objective id is unique across files |
| <a id="format--case-markdown-only-duplicate-rejected"></a>Each file passes, the whole course does not | The same course with `make.md` and `finish.md` both declaring objective `agent-passes`; every file still passes its single-file shape | Course syntax | Rejected | The single-file schema check passes, and the later Markdown check fails because `agent-passes` is declared twice across files |

Source: [spec/format.md](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/format.md).

---

<a id="execution"></a>

## Agent Courses 1.0: execution contract

This page defines how a runner teaches a lesson, runs checks, gathers evidence and settles objectives. It assumes the folder and lesson rules in [the format contract](/specification/#format). Learner feedback is in [the consent contract](/specification/#consent). The record files, call events, receipts and course identity are defined by a later contract; this page states only the rules a runner must follow whatever those files look like. Each rule is listed in [the requirement register](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md).

The words MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD and MAY are used as in [the format contract](/specification/#format--rules-and-advice). A paragraph labelled **Advice** never makes a course or a runner invalid. Examples are cases, built as [the format contract's case tables](/specification/#format--case-tables) describe, starting from [the baseline course](/specification/#format--baseline-course). Wording marked **Proposed** waits for the owner's review on the pull request.

### Who does what <a id="execution--who-does-what"></a>

The tutor does the typing and the learner makes the calls. A runner MAY write and edit project files at the learner's request, because the typing is not what is being learned. A runner MUST NOT make a call the lesson reserves for the learner. An objective settles only when the evidence passed and every call in its lesson was the learner's. [Settlement](/specification/#execution--settlement) states this exactly.

#### Ownership cases <a id="execution--example-ownership"></a>

These cases use the baseline `finish.md`: objective `agent-passes` by `final-check`, and the call `choose-check`.

A call counts as verified only on a host whose confirmation boundary has been qualified separately. This specification qualifies no host, so the accepted cases below describe such a host and every other host leaves the call unverified. See [Settlement](/specification/#execution--settlement).

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-owner-accepted"></a>Learner owns the call, tutor types | `final-check` passed; `choose-check` was put to the learner, answered by the learner and verified as theirs; the tutor typed every edit | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The evidence passed and every call in the lesson was the learner's, so `agent-passes` settles |
| <a id="execution--case-owner-tutor-call-settled-rejected"></a>Tutor call settled | The same evidence, but the tutor decided `choose-check`; the runner records `agent-passes` as settled | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A call decided by the tutor is not the learner's, so the objective stays unsettled |
| <a id="execution--case-owner-typing-accepted"></a>Typing does not move ownership | The tutor typed every edit and the learner still answered `choose-check` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Ownership sits on the call, not on who typed |
| <a id="execution--case-owner-assisted-flag-rejected"></a>An assisted flag decides | The runner settles `agent-passes` because "the tutor wrote no solution code", while `choose-check` was the tutor's | Runner behaviour | Rejected | An assisted flag no longer controls settlement; call ownership does |
| <a id="execution--case-owner-tutor-counted-rejected"></a>Tutor call counted as the learner's | The tutor chose `choose-check`; the runner stores it as the learner's call | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A tutor decision never counts as a learner decision |

#### Capabilities <a id="execution--capabilities"></a>

A runner names what it can do, because what it may settle depends on what it can see.

| Capability | The runner can | So it may record |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `run-checks` | Execute command checks | `checked` |
| `observe` | Inspect project files, Git state and the output of commands it ran | `observed` |
| `judge` | Hold a conversation and apply a rubric | `explained` |

A runner with none of these can deliver a course correctly but settles no objective. Version 1.0 defines no other capability. In particular it defines no `render` capability; a runner MUST NOT claim one as part of 1.0.

### The teaching loop <a id="execution--teaching-loop"></a>

Each lesson moves through the same steps. The wording, examples and pacing are the runner's to choose; the order and the gates are not.

1. **Goal.** The runner says the lesson's `## Goal` text as written.
2. **Build together.** The tutor teaches against the learner's project and builds with them, following `## Build`.
3. **Calls.** Each call the lesson declares is put to the learner. Calls may arise during the build, but [a call comes before the change that depends on it](/specification/#execution--calls-duties).
4. **Check.** The runner runs the lesson's checks through the host's permission model. A lesson with no checks, such as every `understand` lesson, skips this step.
5. **Review.** The runner reports each result with its path and fix, rereads and summarises what changed, and the learner decides whether to continue, revise or skip.
6. **Explain back.** In the last lesson only, after the checks, the learner explains their work and the runner judges it.

A failing check returns the lesson to build and review, as often as the learner wishes. An `understand` lesson declares no checks (see [stages](/specification/#format--stages)), so for it the loop is goal, build together, calls and review, and it settles nothing. A `demonstrate` lesson runs any checks it declares before its explain-back.

#### Loop cases <a id="execution--example-loop"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-loop-accepted"></a>Full loop | Extended baseline. For `make.md`: Goal, build together, the learner's calls, the check `agent-runs`, review. For `finish.md`, the same through review, then the explain-back | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The order is goal, build, calls, check, review, and the explain-back comes last and only in the final lesson |
| <a id="execution--case-loop-check-before-gate-rejected"></a>Check before its gate | Extended baseline `make.md`; the runner runs `agent-runs` straight after the build, before the learner agrees to the first run | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Running a check for the first time is a gate, and the runner waits for it |
| <a id="execution--case-loop-explain-back-omitted-rejected"></a>Final explain-back omitted | Baseline `finish.md`; the runner ends the lesson after review and records the course as complete | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The last lesson ends with the explain-back, after its checks |
| <a id="execution--case-loop-understand-accepted"></a>Understand lesson loop | Baseline `intro.md`: goal, build together, calls and review; no check step | Runner behaviour | Accepted | An understand lesson declares no checks, so the check step is skipped |

#### Gates <a id="execution--gates"></a>

A *gate* is a point where the learner's explicit input is needed to continue. The gates are: starting a lesson after its goal, running a check for the first time, moving on from review, entering the explain-back and skipping anything. A runner MUST wait at every gate. A timer, silence, a check that happened to pass while the learner was still working and "it looks done" are none of them input. A runner MUST NOT supply the learner's input, for example by confirming on their behalf.

##### Gate cases <a id="execution--example-gates"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-start-accepted"></a>Waits after Goal | Baseline `finish.md`; the runner says the Goal and waits; the learner writes "start" | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The gate after the goal is satisfied by the learner's explicit input |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-each-accepted"></a>Waits at a later gate | Extended baseline `make.md`; the runner waits for the learner before the first check run and before moving on from review | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Each declared gate is awaited |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-silence-rejected"></a>Silence advances | The runner starts the build after the learner says nothing | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Silence is not input |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-timer-rejected"></a>A timer advances | The runner moves on from review after ten minutes | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A timer is not input |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-tutor-confirm-rejected"></a>The tutor confirms | The runner writes "yes, start" on the learner's behalf | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT supply the learner's input |

##### Explain-back entry cases <a id="execution--example-gate-explain-back"></a>

These cases use baseline `finish.md` after `final-check` passed and the learner chose to move on from review.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-explain-back-accepted"></a>Learner enters the explain-back | The runner asks "Ready for the explain-back?"; the learner writes "yes, go ahead"; the runner puts the prompt under `## Explain back` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Entering the explain-back is a gate, and the learner's explicit input satisfied it |
| <a id="execution--case-gate-explain-back-rejected"></a>Runner enters without asking | The review gate is satisfied, but the learner has not agreed to start the explain-back; the runner puts the prompt at once | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Entering the explain-back is its own gate, separate from moving on from review, and the runner MUST wait at it |

#### Skipping <a id="execution--skipping"></a>

Moving on without passing is always allowed. The runner records the lesson as skipped, never as passed, and settles none of its objectives.

##### Skipping cases <a id="execution--example-skipping"></a>

These cases use the extended baseline with `make.md` gaining the objective `agent-runs-ok` (`by: agent-runs`). In the trace, the observe check `agent-runs` last returned `fail`, because `agent.py` does not exist, and the learner chooses to move on at the review gate.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-skip-accepted"></a>Skip recorded as skipped | The trace above; the runner records `make` as skipped and `agent-runs-ok` as unsettled | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Moving on without passing is skipped, and a skipped lesson settles nothing |
| <a id="execution--case-skip-recorded-passed-rejected"></a>Skip recorded as passed | The identical trace; the runner records `make` as passed | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The lesson did not pass, so it cannot be recorded as passed |
| <a id="execution--case-skip-objective-settled-rejected"></a>Skip settles the objective | The identical trace; the runner records `make` as skipped and `agent-runs-ok` as settled | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A skipped lesson settles none of its objectives |

#### Help <a id="execution--help-ladder"></a>

A runner reveals help in steps: one hint per request, then targeted re-teaching, then a worked example only if the learner asks. Hints come from `## Hints` in order. Help from the tutor is never counted as an attempt, and it is never evidence that the learner owned a decision, including when a session resumes.

##### Help cases <a id="execution--example-help"></a>

These cases use a lesson with three hints under `## Hints`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-help-one-hint-accepted"></a>One hint per request | The learner asks for a hint; the runner shows hint 1 only | Runner behaviour | Accepted | One hint per request, in order |
| <a id="execution--case-help-all-hints-rejected"></a>All hints at once | The learner asks for a hint; the runner shows hints 1 to 3 | Runner behaviour | Rejected | More than one hint was revealed for one request |
| <a id="execution--case-help-reteach-accepted"></a>Re-teach after the hints | After three hints the learner asks again; the runner re-teaches the idea against their project | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Targeted re-teaching follows the hints |
| <a id="execution--case-help-unsolicited-solution-rejected"></a>Unsolicited solution | The runner shows a worked example before the learner asked | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A worked example is given only if the learner asks |
| <a id="execution--case-help-worked-example-accepted"></a>Requested worked example | The learner asks for a worked example and the runner shows it | Runner behaviour | Accepted | It is help, not an attempt and not evidence of learner ownership |
| <a id="execution--case-help-attempt-rejected"></a>Counted as an attempt | The runner counts that worked example as an attempt | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Help is never an attempt |
| <a id="execution--case-help-ownership-rejected"></a>Counted as ownership | The runner treats the learner reading the worked example as their decision for call `choose-check` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Help is never evidence that the learner owned a decision |

### Runner duties <a id="execution--runner-duties"></a>

#### Preparing the project <a id="execution--project-preparation"></a>

Before the first lesson the runner prepares the project root in a fixed order. The rules for each kind are in [the format contract](/specification/#format--projects).

1. Check the `project` syntax. A malformed value stops the course.
2. If the list holds two or more options, wait for the learner's choice. A runner MUST NOT choose for them.
3. For a Git reference, resolve the pin and check its `project:` links against the immutable checkout. For a local folder (**proposed**), check the source and its `project:` links against the selected subtree of the installed course snapshot. A failure rejects this project's start.
4. For a local folder (**proposed**), obtain an editable destination from the learner or embedding application, refuse a non-empty destination, then copy the source into it. The runner MUST NOT run project code while copying. For `starter`, create the new folder from `starter/` under the same non-reuse rule.
5. Begin lesson one with the destination as the project root.

On resume the runner uses the existing project root and the original snapshot. It MUST NOT copy a local source again or move a pin. At every open of a `project:` link it resolves against the editable project root and warns when it cannot.

##### Preparation cases <a id="execution--example-preparation"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-local-order-accepted"></a>Local order | `project: ./examples/app`; the runner validates the source subtree and its `README.md#L10-L40` link, takes `work/local-app`, copies, then says the first Goal | Project start | Accepted | Validation precedes the copy and the copy precedes lesson one |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-copy-after-lesson-rejected"></a>Copy after lesson one begins | The same course; the runner says the first Goal, then copies | Project start | Rejected | Materialisation happens before the first lesson |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-validate-after-copy-rejected"></a>Validation after the copy | The same course; the runner copies, then finds `README.md` has 9 lines | Project start | Rejected | Baseline links are checked before the selected project starts |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-run-code-rejected"></a>Project code runs during the copy | The source holds `setup.sh`; the runner runs it while copying | Project start | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT run project code while copying |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-git-order-accepted"></a>Git order | `gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0`; the runner resolves the tag, checks `README.md#L10` against the pinned checkout, then starts | Project start | Accepted | The pin is resolved and baseline links are checked before the start |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-resume-accepted"></a>Resume uses the workspace | A local-folder course resumes at lesson `make`; the runner uses `work/local-app` and the installed snapshot | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Resume uses the existing workspace and original snapshot |
| <a id="execution--case-prep-resume-recopy-rejected"></a>Resume recopies | The same resume; the runner copies the source into `work/local-app` again | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Resume MUST NOT copy the source again |

##### Git pin on resume cases <a id="execution--example-pin-resume"></a>

These cases use `gh:example-co/payments` with no `@`. The runner resolved the default branch once and recorded commit `9f3c2e1a4b5d6c7e8f90a1b2c3d4e5f607182930`. The branch has since advanced to commit `7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a7a`. No payload claims a name or version in these cases.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-pin-resume-accepted"></a>Resume keeps the recorded commit | The learner resumes; the runner uses the checkout at `9f3c2e1a…` and does not look up the branch | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Resume uses the original snapshot and does not move the pin |
| <a id="execution--case-pin-resume-moved-rejected"></a>Resume moves the pin | The learner resumes; the runner resolves the default branch again, finds `7a7a7a7a…` and checks it out in place of the recorded commit | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Resume MUST NOT move a pin, whether or not any payload claims a name or version |

#### Calls <a id="execution--calls-duties"></a>

For each call a lesson declares, a runner:

- MUST put the call to the learner, using the call's `ask` as the question;
- MAY recommend an option and say what it costs;
- MUST NOT decide the call itself;
- MUST wait for the learner's answer before making any change that depends on it.

If the learner explicitly asks the tutor to choose, the tutor MAY decide, and that call is a tutor decision. A call that was never put to the learner is not the learner's. A tutor decision never counts as a learner decision, and the lesson may still finish without settling its objectives.

Whether a call counts as the learner's is further limited by what the runner can prove. [Settlement](/specification/#execution--settlement) applies the owner-approved capability contract on learner-decision receipts.

##### Call duty cases <a id="execution--example-calls-duties"></a>

These cases use the baseline call `choose-tool` with `ask: Which tool will your agent call first?`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-calls-put-accepted"></a>Put, recommend, wait | The runner asks "Which tool will your agent call first?", offers `search` and `calculator` with their trade-offs, recommends `search` and waits | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The call was put with its `ask`; a recommendation is allowed; the runner waited |
| <a id="execution--case-calls-decided-first-rejected"></a>Runner chooses first | The runner picks `search` and edits `agent.py` before the learner answers | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT decide the call and MUST wait for the answer before the dependent change |
| <a id="execution--case-calls-delegated-accepted"></a>Learner delegates | The learner says "you pick"; the tutor chooses `search` and records the call as the tutor's; the objectives it affects stay open | Runner behaviour | Accepted | An explicit request lets the tutor decide, and that is a tutor decision |
| <a id="execution--case-calls-delegated-as-learner-rejected"></a>Delegated call recorded as the learner's | The same exchange; the runner records `choose-tool` as the learner's, or settles from it | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A tutor decision never counts as a learner decision |
| <a id="execution--case-calls-never-put-rejected"></a>Never put | The runner makes the choice silently and later records the call as answered | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A call that was never put to the learner is not the learner's |

#### Building together <a id="execution--build-duties"></a>

A runner:

- MUST show each change it makes to a project file and say why, as it makes it;
- MUST reread an existing project file immediately before it changes that file, so that a hand edit by the learner is never overwritten;
- MUST NOT invent a fact about the learner's project, such as a number, a date, a name or a cost. It uses only what it read in the project, what a command printed, or what the learner said, and otherwise asks;
- MUST NOT change a file the learner did not ask it to change while running a check or giving a hint;
- MUST teach against the learner's project where the lesson allows, rather than reciting the lesson text;
- MUST NOT say any lesson section other than `## Goal` as written, and MUST NOT show tutor-only material such as rubrics, quiz keys and misconception notes;
- MUST NOT send code, file contents or conversation anywhere through a hook;
- MUST NOT create, edit, move or delete a project file during an explain-back ([silence](/specification/#execution--explain-back-silence)).

**Advice.** Write lessons as instructions to the tutor in the imperative, such as "Ask the learner which option they prefer, then draft the section". Only `## Goal` is addressed to the learner.

##### Building duty cases <a id="execution--example-runner-duties"></a>

These cases use the project file `plan/PLAN.md`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-build-duties-accepted"></a>All four duties | The runner shows the diff of an edit to `plan/PLAN.md` and says why; rereads the file after the learner added a line by hand and keeps that line; writes "the project has 3 open issues" only after reading three issues; and makes no file edit during the explain-back | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The change is shown with a reason, the file was reread before the edit, the fact came from the project, and the explain-back is silent |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-hidden-edit-rejected"></a>Edit not shown | The runner edits `plan/PLAN.md` without saying what changed or why | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Each change MUST be shown with a reason |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-stale-read-rejected"></a>Stale read | The runner edits `plan/PLAN.md` from a copy read earlier, after the learner added a line | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The runner MUST reread immediately before changing the file; the edit may overwrite the learner |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-invented-fact-rejected"></a>Invented date | The runner writes a launch date the learner never gave and the project does not hold | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT invent a fact; it asks instead |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-explain-back-edit-rejected"></a>Explain-back file edit | A project file is edited while the explain-back is open | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT write to the project during an explain-back |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-record-write-accepted"></a>Runner record write | The runner appends to its own record under `.agents/` during the explain-back | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Only project files are protected; the runner's own record is exempt |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-options-accepted"></a>Offers options and waits | The runner offers two options and a recommendation, then waits | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A recommendation is allowed and the learner decides |
| <a id="execution--case-duty-picks-rejected"></a>Picks and carries on | The runner picks the option and carries on without asking | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT decide the call |

##### Tutor edit and privacy cases <a id="execution--example-tutor-edits"></a>

These cases use a project holding `plan/PLAN.md` and `plan/NOTES.md`. The learner has asked for no edit to either file. Here the actor is the tutor, not the check program.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-check-untouched-accepted"></a>Check run leaves files alone | The tutor runs `final-check` and then reports the result; `plan/PLAN.md` and `plan/NOTES.md` are byte for byte unchanged | Runner behaviour | Accepted | No unrequested file changed while running a check |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-check-edit-rejected"></a>Check run reformats a note | While running `final-check` the tutor also reformats `plan/NOTES.md`; nothing else changes | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT change a file the learner did not ask it to change while running a check |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-hint-untouched-accepted"></a>Hint is spoken only | The tutor gives hint 1 in words; no project file changes | Runner behaviour | Accepted | No unrequested file changed while giving a hint |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-hint-edit-rejected"></a>Hint applies itself | While giving hint 1 the tutor adds the hinted line to `plan/NOTES.md`; nothing else changes | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT change a file the learner did not ask it to change while giving a hint |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-hint-requested-edit-accepted"></a>Learner asks for the edit | After hint 1 the learner writes "please apply that to PLAN.md"; the tutor shows the change, says why and edits `plan/PLAN.md` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The learner asked for the change, so the duty is not breached |

##### Tutor-only material cases <a id="execution--example-tutor-only"></a>

These cases use `tutor/finish.rubric.md` (`pass: 2 of 3` and the three criteria), the quiz key in `tutor/make.quiz.yaml` (both entries `answer: 1`) and a note `tutor/misconceptions.md` reading "Learners think tools are registered on the model". All values are illustrative.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-only-hidden-accepted"></a>Material stays with the tutor | The runner puts the explain-back prompt without listing the criteria, asks quiz question 1 without showing the key, and after the answer explains in its own words that tools are registered on the agent object | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The explanation is the runner's own wording, and no rubric, key or note is shown |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-only-rubric-rejected"></a>Rubric shown | Before the learner answers the explain-back, the runner pastes the three criteria from `tutor/finish.rubric.md` to the learner | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT show a rubric |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-only-key-rejected"></a>Quiz key shown | Before the learner answers question 1, the runner shows the entry `answer: 1` from `tutor/make.quiz.yaml` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT show a quiz key |
| <a id="execution--case-tutor-only-note-rejected"></a>Misconception note shown | The runner reads "Learners think tools are registered on the model" from `tutor/misconceptions.md` to the learner as a note | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT show a misconception note |

##### Explain-back operation cases <a id="execution--example-explain-back-ops"></a>

These cases use baseline `finish.md` with the explain-back prompt open. The project holds `agent.py` and `tests/test_agent.py`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-explain-ops-spoken-accepted"></a>Spoken retry and record write | The learner's first attempt misses a criterion; the tutor gives targeted help in words and the learner tries again; the runner appends to its own file under `.agents/`; no project file changes | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Retries stay spoken, and the runner's own record under `.agents/` is permitted |
| <a id="execution--case-explain-ops-create-rejected"></a>File created | During the explain-back the tutor creates `notes.md` in the project | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT create a project file during an explain-back |
| <a id="execution--case-explain-ops-move-rejected"></a>File moved | During the explain-back the tutor moves `agent.py` to `old/agent.py` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT move a project file during an explain-back |
| <a id="execution--case-explain-ops-delete-rejected"></a>File deleted | During the explain-back the tutor deletes `tests/test_agent.py` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The tutor MUST NOT delete a project file during an explain-back |
| <a id="execution--case-explain-ops-retry-edit-rejected"></a>Retry done by editing | After targeted help the tutor retries by editing `agent.py` to show the fix instead of speaking it | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Retries after targeted help stay spoken; editing a project file breaches the silence |

#### Silence during explain-back <a id="execution--explain-back-silence"></a>

From the moment the explain-back prompt is put until the attempt is judged or the learner leaves the step, the tutor MUST NOT create, edit, move or delete any project file. This includes retries after targeted help, which stay spoken. The runner MAY write its own record files under `.agents/`. A learner who wants to change the project leaves the explain-back, returns to build and review, and starts a new explain-back later.

#### Resuming <a id="execution--resuming"></a>

A runner MUST be able to resume a learner at the same lesson and step from the record alone, with no conversation history. A call that was open when the session ended is put again; a runner MUST NOT infer that it was answered.

#### Celebrating <a id="execution--celebrating"></a>

A runner celebrates only with facts it was given: the course name from `COURSE.md`, and a URL or handle only from the `homepage` and `handle` metadata keys. Otherwise it celebrates plainly.

##### Resume and celebration cases <a id="execution--example-resume-celebrate"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-resume-accepted"></a>Resume at the step | The session ends at lesson `make`, step check; the next session reads the record alone and starts at that lesson and step with no chat | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Resume needs only the record |
| <a id="execution--case-resume-conversation-rejected"></a>Resume needs the chat | The next session asks the learner to recall where they were from memory of the chat | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Resume must not rely on conversation |
| <a id="execution--case-resume-open-call-accepted"></a>Open call asked again | The session ended while the call `choose-tool` was open; the runner puts it again | Runner behaviour | Accepted | An open call is put again |
| <a id="execution--case-resume-open-call-inferred-rejected"></a>Answer inferred from a file | The runner assumes `choose-tool` was answered because `tools.py` now exists | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT infer that an open call was answered |
| <a id="execution--case-celebrate-homepage-accepted"></a>Supplied homepage | `homepage` is `https://example.com`; the runner closes with "You finished Sample course. Read more at https://example.com" | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The course name and URL came from `COURSE.md` |
| <a id="execution--case-celebrate-handle-absent-accepted"></a>Absent handle omitted | `homepage` and `handle` are absent; the runner closes with "You finished Sample course" | Runner behaviour | Accepted | It uses only given facts |
| <a id="execution--case-celebrate-invented-rejected"></a>Invented handle | `homepage` and `handle` are absent; the runner closes with "@example-team will be proud" | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT invent a homepage or handle |
| <a id="execution--case-celebrate-invented-homepage-rejected"></a>Invented homepage | The same course; the runner closes with "Read more at https://example.org" | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT invent a homepage |

### Checks <a id="execution--checks"></a>

A check is a small program, or an inspection, that looks at the learner's project and says pass or fail.

#### Check declarations <a id="execution--check-declarations"></a>

Checks are declared in `checks/checks.yaml` as a list of mappings.

```yaml
- id: tool-is-registered
  run: python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/tool_is_registered.py"
  verify: The agent registers at least one tool the model can call
  timeout: 30
  network: false
- id: output-type-is-used
  kind: observe
  verify: agent.py sets an output type on the agent and uses its fields
```

| Field | Required | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | Yes | A kebab id, unique among checks, and never the reserved word `explain-back`. Every id a lesson lists MUST exist here. A check that no lesson lists is allowed, and a validator warns. |
| `kind` | No | `command` (the default) or `observe`. |
| `run` | Command checks | A command line, as below. An observe check MUST NOT have `run`. |
| `verify` | Yes | One sentence saying what passing means. The program decides; this sentence is the contract that the fixtures test. |
| `timeout` | No | A whole number of seconds from 1 to 300. Default 60. A larger or non-integer value is invalid. |
| `network` | No | A boolean. Default `false`. |

##### Check declaration cases <a id="execution--example-check-declarations"></a>

These cases start from the baseline `checks.yaml`, with `final-check` as the command check and the extended baseline's observe check `agent-runs`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-check-shape-accepted"></a>Valid shapes | `final-check` with `run`, `verify`, `timeout: 1`, then `timeout: 300`, then no `timeout` (which means 60), and `network: true`; `agent-runs` with `kind: observe` and `verify` only | Course syntax | Accepted | A command has `run` and `verify`, an observe has `verify` only, the timeout is a whole number from 1 to 300 or omitted, and `network` is boolean |
| <a id="execution--case-check-id-duplicate-rejected"></a>Repeated check id | `checks.yaml` lists `final-check` twice, each entry complete with `run`, `verify`, `timeout` and `network`; the one set of `final-check` fixtures exists | Course syntax | Rejected | Check ids are unique among checks. Both entries are otherwise valid, so only the repeat decides. Finding it means comparing the `id` property after parsing, because two entries with other differing fields are still different as whole items |
| <a id="execution--case-check-run-missing-rejected"></a>Command without run | `final-check` without `run` | Course syntax | Rejected | A command check MUST have `run` |
| <a id="execution--case-check-verify-missing-rejected"></a>Check without verify | `final-check` without `verify` | Course syntax | Rejected | `verify` is required |
| <a id="execution--case-check-observe-run-rejected"></a>Observe with run | `agent-runs` with a `run` line added | Course syntax | Rejected | An observe check MUST NOT have `run` |
| <a id="execution--case-check-timeout-zero-rejected"></a>Timeout 0 | `final-check` with `timeout: 0` | Course syntax | Rejected | The timeout is at least 1 |
| <a id="execution--case-check-timeout-high-rejected"></a>Timeout 301 | `final-check` with `timeout: 301` | Course syntax | Rejected | The timeout is at most 300 |
| <a id="execution--case-check-timeout-fraction-rejected"></a>Fractional timeout | `final-check` with `timeout: 1.5` | Course syntax | Rejected | The timeout is a whole number |
| <a id="execution--case-check-network-type-rejected"></a>Network not boolean | `final-check` with `network: "yes"` | Course syntax | Rejected | `network` is a boolean |
| <a id="execution--case-check-unlisted-warning"></a>Declared but unused | `checks.yaml` declares a complete command check `spare-check` with `run`, `verify`, `pass/` and `fail/` fixtures, and no lesson lists it | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | A check that no lesson lists is allowed, and a validator warns |

#### Where a command runs <a id="execution--check-paths"></a>

A command check runs with **the project root as its working directory**. The project root is the editable project the learner works in: the current project for `own`, the new folder for `starter`, the checkout (and its `#` folder) for a Git reference and, **proposed**, the editable destination copy for a local folder. The runner sets two environment variables:

- `AGENT_COURSE_DIR`: the absolute path of the installed, read-only course folder;
- `AGENT_COURSE_PROJECT`: the absolute path of the project root.

A relative path in `run` therefore always means the learner's project. A course file MUST be named through `AGENT_COURSE_DIR`. The expansion SHOULD be written in double quotes, because the course folder's name may contain spaces; this is **Advice**. The command line is interpreted by the host's command interpreter. A runner MUST substitute both variables as written, and an author who needs portability avoids interpreter-specific syntax.

##### Path cases <a id="execution--example-check-paths"></a>

These cases use `run` lines for the baseline course installed at `/home/ana/courses/sample-course`, with the learner's workspace at `/home/ana/work`. For the local-folder case the project is `./examples/app`, copied to `/home/ana/work/work/local-app`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-project-accepted"></a>Project is the working directory | `own` project in `/home/ana/work`; `run: python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py"`; the runner starts it with working directory `/home/ana/work`, `AGENT_COURSE_DIR=/home/ana/courses/sample-course` and `AGENT_COURSE_PROJECT=/home/ana/work` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The working directory is the project root; the course script is reached through the variable |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-local-accepted"></a>Local folder working directory | `project: ./examples/app` copied to `work/local-app`; the same `run` line; working directory and `AGENT_COURSE_PROJECT` are `/home/ana/work/work/local-app`; `AGENT_COURSE_DIR` stays `/home/ana/courses/sample-course` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The project root is the editable copy, and the installed course stays read-only |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-relative-project-accepted"></a>Relative path means the project | `run: test -f agent.py` | Course syntax | Accepted | It tests `agent.py` in the project root, because relative means project |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-course-as-cwd-rejected"></a>Course folder as working directory | The runner starts `final-check` with working directory `/home/ana/courses/sample-course` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A command runs with the project root as its working directory |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-unquoted-warning"></a>Unquoted expansion | `run: python $AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py` | Advisory lint | Accepted with a warning | Quoting is advice; it works until the course path contains a space |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-relative-course-script-error"></a>Relative course script | `run: python checks/final_check.py` | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | A validator may warn after finding `checks/final_check.py` in the course; at run time the interpreter looks in the project, cannot find the file and exits 2, so the runner reports a broken check, not a runtime failure of the learner |
| <a id="execution--case-cwd-source-edit-rejected"></a>Check run against the source | `project: ./examples/app`; the runner uses `examples/app` in the installed course as `AGENT_COURSE_PROJECT` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The project root of a local folder is the destination copy, not the source |

#### Showing the command <a id="execution--command-display"></a>

Before the first run of each distinct `run` string in a project, the runner MUST show the learner the command exactly as written, the working directory, and the values of both variables, and let the learner decline. A declined check is recorded as not run. It is neither passed nor failed, and it settles nothing. A changed `run` string is shown again.

##### Command display cases <a id="execution--example-command-display"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-display-before-accepted"></a>Shown first | Before the first run the runner shows `python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py"`, the working directory and both variable values, and waits | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The exact command, directory and variables precede the first run |
| <a id="execution--case-display-after-rejected"></a>Shown afterwards | The runner runs the command first and shows it afterwards | Runner behaviour | Rejected | It MUST be shown before the first run |
| <a id="execution--case-display-declined-accepted"></a>Declined | The learner declines; the runner records not run and settles nothing from that check | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A declined check is not run |
| <a id="execution--case-display-declined-failed-rejected"></a>Declined recorded as failed | The learner declines; the runner records the check as failed | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Declined is neither passed nor failed |
| <a id="execution--case-display-repeat-accepted"></a>Same string again | The same `run` string runs a second time in the same project | Runner behaviour | Accepted | It is not shown again |
| <a id="execution--case-display-changed-rejected"></a>Changed string not shown | A course upgrade changes the `run` string; the runner runs it without showing it | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A changed string is shown again |

#### Permission, time and network <a id="execution--host-permission"></a>

Any executable the host permits may be used. A course MAY call a script in any language, a built-in command or an installed tool. The spec imposes no language list. A runner MUST run commands through the host's own permission model and MUST NOT work around a refusal by another route. A command the host refuses is recorded as not run.

A runner:

- MUST stop a command at its timeout; a timeout counts as a broken check, never as a failure of the learner's work;
- SHOULD run checks with the project read-only, a temporary scratch directory, the declared timeout and no network;
- MAY refuse a check with `network: true`, and a refused check is recorded as not run.

##### Host permission cases <a id="execution--example-host-permission"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-host-python-accepted"></a>Permitted script | `run: python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/a.py"` where the host permits Python | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A host-permitted executable may be used |
| <a id="execution--case-host-gradle-accepted"></a>Any language | `run: ./gradlew test` where the host permits that tool | Runner behaviour | Accepted | No language list applies |
| <a id="execution--case-host-denied-accepted"></a>Denied command | `run: ./gradlew test` where the host permission prompt is declined; recorded as not run | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A command the host refuses is not run, and nothing settles |
| <a id="execution--case-host-workaround-rejected"></a>Work around a refusal | The host refuses the command and the runner runs it through another tool | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST NOT work around a refusal by another route |
| <a id="execution--case-host-timeout-accepted"></a>Timeout | A command exceeds `timeout: 30` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `error`, never a learner failure |
| <a id="execution--case-host-timeout-failure-rejected"></a>Timeout counted against the learner | The same timeout; the runner records the check as `fail` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A timeout counts as a broken check, never as a failure of the learner's work |
| <a id="execution--case-host-network-refused-accepted"></a>Network refused | `network: true` and the runner refuses it; recorded as not run | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A runner MAY refuse a check with `network: true` |
| <a id="execution--case-host-network-undeclared-accepted"></a>Undeclared network need | A command needs the network, declares `network: false` and exits 2 with the network off | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `error`, and the author must declare the network |

##### Runner isolation cases <a id="execution--example-runner-isolation"></a>

These cases judge how the runner sets up a check, not what the check program does. They use `final-check` with `timeout: 30` and `network: false`, run on the baseline project. A deviation from a SHOULD is accepted with a warning, or accepted without one when the runner states a justified exception to the learner.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-accepted"></a>Runner sets up as advised | The runner makes the project read-only, gives the check a temporary scratch directory, stops it at 30 seconds and turns the network off | Runner behaviour | Accepted | All four advised settings are applied |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-writable-warning"></a>Project left writable | The same run, except the project is writable; the runner gives no reason | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | The project SHOULD be read-only; a deviation without a reason warns and never invalidates the runner or the course |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-scratch-warning"></a>No scratch directory | The same run, except no scratch directory is provided; the runner gives no reason | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | A temporary scratch directory is advised |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-network-warning"></a>Network left on | The same run, except the network is on although `network: false`; the runner gives no reason | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | No network is advised when the check declares none |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-network-declared-accepted"></a>Network declared | `final-check` declares `network: true`; the runner leaves the network on | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The advice is no network unless declared, and here it is declared |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-exception-accepted"></a>Justified exception | The host cannot make the project read-only; the runner tells the learner this before the run and proceeds with the other settings | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A stated, justified exception to the advice is not a deviation |
| <a id="execution--case-isolation-timeout-rejected"></a>Declared timeout not applied | The runner lets the same command run for 100 seconds although it declares `timeout: 30` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST stop a command at its timeout; this one is a MUST, not advice |

#### Check results <a id="execution--check-results"></a>

The exit code is the whole contract.

| Exit code | Result | Means | Counts against the learner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `pass` | The requirement is met | No |
| 1 | `fail` | The requirement is not met and the learner can fix it | Yes, as a failure |
| Any other, including a timeout, a signal, a missing program (126 or 127) and a refused write | `error` | The check itself is broken | Never |

A check MAY also write one JSON object per line to standard output, and a runner SHOULD show it. The fields are `check` (the id), `status` (`pass`, `fail` or `error`), `path` (relative to the project root, never absolute), `line` (a positive integer), `message` and `fix`. If a line contradicts the exit code, the exit code wins and the contradiction is a defect in the check. A runner reports an `error` with an apology and never settles anything on it.

##### Result cases <a id="execution--example-exit-codes"></a>

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-zero-accepted"></a>Exit 0 | `final-check` exits 0 | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The runner result is `pass` |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-one-accepted"></a>Exit 1 with JSON | Exit 1 and a JSON line `{"check":"final-check","status":"fail","path":"agent.py","line":12,"message":"No tool is registered.","fix":"Decorate a function with the tool decorator."}` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `fail`, shown with path, message and fix |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-two-accepted"></a>Exit 2 | `final-check` exits 2 | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `error`; nothing is held against the learner |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-127-accepted"></a>Exit 127 | The program is not found and exits 127 | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `error` |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-timeout-accepted"></a>Killed at its timeout | `final-check` is stopped at its timeout | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The result is `error` |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-json-conflict-accepted"></a>JSON contradicts exit 0 | Exit 0 with a JSON line whose status is `fail` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The exit code wins; the result is `pass` and a defect in the check is reported |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-two-learner-fail-rejected"></a>Exit 2 as a learner failure | `final-check` exits 2 and the runner records `fail` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Any code other than 0 or 1 is `error` and never counts against the learner |
| <a id="execution--case-exit-json-override-rejected"></a>JSON overrides exit 1 | Exit 1 with a JSON line whose status is `pass`; the runner records `pass` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The exit code wins over a conflicting line |

##### Optional diagnostic display cases <a id="execution--example-json-display"></a>

These cases use `final-check` exiting 1 with the JSON line `{"check":"final-check","status":"fail","path":"agent.py","line":12,"message":"No tool is registered.","fix":"Decorate a function with the tool decorator."}`.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="execution--case-json-shown-accepted"></a>Diagnostics shown | The runner shows `agent.py`, line 12, the message and the fix, and records `fail` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The valid line is shown and the exit code decides the result |
| <a id="execution--case-json-omitted-warning"></a>Diagnostics left out | The runner records `fail` and tells the learner only that the check failed, without showing the valid line | Runner behaviour | Accepted with a warning | A runner SHOULD show valid diagnostics; the exit code alone still gives `fail`, so the result is unchanged |
