A `declined` change records that a hook was skipped or refused for a lesson, so a runner that resumes mid-lesson does not ask again, as [the consent contract](/specification/#consent--consent-rules) requires. No destination, payload or answer is stored beyond these names, and silence writes nothing, because silence is a no. Withdrawal also discards unsent payloads, which this file never holds.

### The course lock <a id="records--lock-file"></a>

`.agents/courses.lock` is one JSON object. A team commits it, so it holds no learner data.

```json
{
  "spec": "1.0",
  "courses": [
    {
      "name": "sample-course",
      "version": "1.0.0",
      "source": "gh:example-co/courses@v1.0.0#sample-course",
      "commit": "9f3c2e1a4b5d6c7e8f90a1b2c3d4e5f607182930",
      "identity": "sha256:<64 hexadecimal digits>"
    }
  ]
}
```

Each entry has `name` (the course name, which is also its folder name), `version`, `source` (see below), `identity` and, for a Git source only, `commit`. Names are unique, and entries are sorted by the bytes of the name. `source` is the string `local` when the learner installed a local folder, and otherwise the Git or HTTPS reference as the learner gave it, at most 2048 characters and credential-free as [the reference grammar](/specification/#format--reference-grammar) requires. A local path is never stored: it would put a user name or home folder into a committed file, and a teammate cannot use it. The installed copy and its identity are what resume needs. A learner who wants to reinstall a local folder names the folder again, and its identity is checked as [pinning](/specification/#identity--pinning) says. The lock holds no timestamp, so installing the same course twice writes the same bytes. The runner replaces it atomically, as [atomic replacement](/specification/#records--atomic-record) describes. A reader ignores unknown fields. The identity duties are in [pinning](/specification/#identity--pinning).

#### Lock field shapes <a id="records--lock-fields"></a>

| Field | Required shape | Source and malformed outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Root `spec` | Exactly string `1.0` | Fixed contract version; any other value refuses the file |
| Root `courses` | Required list, possibly empty; no fixed maximum | One entry per installed course; duplicated names or wrong ordering refuse the file |
| Entry `name` | Required identifier, matching retained directory and course name | Verified authored metadata |
| Entry `version` | Required string under the course version grammar | Verified authored metadata |
| Entry `source` | Required string with transition-source constraints | Last committed transition, or the verified install before first start |
| Entry `commit` | Git-only required hexadecimal string with transition-commit constraints; otherwise forbidden | Resolved install commit preserved by the transition |
| Entry `identity` | Required identity string | Recomputed retained target identity; never accepted from a folder label alone |

Malformed locks do not authorise teaching. Recovery may replace the affected entry from complete committed history and verified authored sources. Other courses' entries MUST remain intact; if they cannot be preserved trustworthily, stop rather than guess. Unknown non-private fields are ignored, except the explicitly forbidden `generation` field.

### Replay <a id="records--replay"></a>

Replay processes complete protected history in file order. It loads authored folders only for transitions that pass the preliminary predicates below. The same complete inputs and original attestation decisions give one projection. A missing required source or authority stops replay without publishing any part of that projection.

#### Retained sources <a id="records--replay-data"></a>

The runner MUST retain each applied start/upgrade folder under `.agents/course-snapshots/<digits>/<course>/` for its event history's lifetime. It copies only identity-included entries, preserving original file bytes, literal link text and empty directories without following links. It excludes Git metadata, generated output, runner state and any learner workspace or private answers. These are authored course files outside `.agents/records/`, not a record payload.

Before constructing a path, require `sha256:` and exactly 64 lowercase hex digits; remove only that prefix for `<digits>`. Require the validated course identifier as the final directory component. Refuse symlink archive components, traversal and any path escaping the retained store. A correctly labelled folder is not proof of its contents.

Before use, recompute the [existing identity](/specification/#identity--identity-digest) and require equality with the transition. Then validate the complete course and its relationships under the [format](/specification/#format) and [execution](/specification/#execution) contracts, including directory name, course name and version. Hashing and metadata interpretation MUST read the same stable bytes under the protected runner's write boundary. A concurrent change aborts the operation. Read-only permission bits alone are insufficient; tutor control of the log, source verification or verifier cannot establish qualified provenance.

Metadata links MUST resolve wholly within the verified, identity-included tree. Escaping, excluded, cyclic or missing targets make the required source unusable. Identity itself still hashes literal link text without following it. Interpret verified text with the identity text rule once; copying retained folders never rewrites source bytes.

| Transient view | Exact source, type and cardinality | Malformed outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Course name, version, lesson order, project choices | One valid `COURSE.md`; name/version strings; at least one unique ordered lesson id; omitted project means `[own]` | Stop required-source replay |
| Stage, checks, calls, objectives | One valid listed lesson file per id; stage enumeration; check references with default-true boolean `required`; unique call ids; ordered objective `{id, by}` pairs; omitted calls/objectives mean empty | Stop required-source replay |
| Check kind and command | Unique declarations in `checks/checks.yaml`; default `command` or `observe`; command-only required `run` string; every referenced id resolves | Stop required-source replay |
| Explain-back threshold | Exactly the final lesson's rubric; integers `K`, `N` from `K of N`, `1 ≤ K ≤ N`; N equals top-level criteria count | Stop required-source replay |

These views are transient, never persisted independent authorities. Unknown author fields retain the ignore-and-warn policy and cannot change derived fields. There is no replay JSON cache, replay manifest, ninth schema or migration for this unpublished candidate. A locally restored exact folder may be reused only after identity and structural verification; never fetch or substitute latest content automatically.

#### Applying events <a id="records--applying-events"></a>

Parse each line's structure first. Invalid lines are skipped and reported under [damage](/specification/#records--damage). For structurally valid lines, evaluate these predicates in order; a refused predicate changes no projection field. A report distinguishes invalid, not applied, and a stopped replay.

1. An unknown kind does nothing, and ignored fields never trigger lookup. Remember occurrences on structurally valid verified decision lines for the existing one-use rule, even when a later predicate refuses that line. This requires no folder or receipt verification.
2. A first `course-started` requires its retained folder, verified name and version. Apply it with generation one. A later start is not applied and does not look up its snapshot. Before an applied start, every other known kind is not applied.
3. For `course-upgraded`, compare `from` with the active identity and require `to != from`. Failure is not applied without target lookup. A line passing these predicates is a committed transition: require its verified target, unchanged course name, matching version and compatible selected project option. Failure to load or validate that commitment stops replay, never converts it into a refused live request.
4. For every lesson-progress event, compare both `snapshot` and `generation` with current state before lookup or receipt verification. Either mismatch is not applied. Generation derives only from applied transitions: zero before start, one after start, then one more for each applied upgrade. Lost or truncated committed history stops authoritative resume; it never resets this counter.
5. A named lesson, call or check must be declared by the current verified course and named lesson. Every lesson event except `lesson-started` also needs an already-started entry. No other kind creates that entry. Consent decline requires a declared lesson but does not create or require progress in it.
6. A `call-decided` needs an open call. A verified occurrence seen on an earlier structurally valid verified line is not applied, even if that earlier line was not applied. For an otherwise applicable learner decision, use the [verification contract](/specification/#records--replay-interface); a rejected result leaves the call open, and unverified never settles anything.
7. A passing/failing `check-run` needs the kind's evidence: checked for command, observed for observe. `explain-judged` needs the final lesson and the verified rubric denominator. A different kind or denominator is not applied.
8. A project can be chosen only once and must match a verified offered option. Completion and skip obey their specific rules below. Other applicable kinds update only the fields assigned to them.

An applied `lesson-started` sets status started unless already passed, removes an earlier skip time, and sets position to that lesson at goal. Checks replace `last` and increment `runs` only for pass/fail. Explain-back replaces the latest attempt, including a miss. Hints, invalid lines and unknown kinds never create attempts. An applied upgrade archives and resets exactly as [upgrades](/specification/#records--upgrades) states. A missing, unreadable, changed or malformed required snapshot stops with its identity and reason: no partial projection, lock update, teaching or settlement follows.

#### Protected admission and restart <a id="records--operation-admission"></a>

Before dispatching a check, requesting an explain-back judgement or presenting a call, the protected runner captures current snapshot and generation. It binds them to that operation, its lesson and declared check/call or explain attempt. Callers MUST NOT choose or replace this binding. When the result arrives, compare its original binding with current state under the writer lock used by upgrades, before appending progress. Require the current snapshot/generation and the existing lesson-start, declared-id and attempt rules. Refuse stale results without appending new progress; never relabel them with current values.

The durable upgrade commit invalidates all earlier admissions, even if their processes continue or the same snapshot returns later. After restart, cancel unfinished check/explain operations and require fresh dispatch. Reconstruct no admission from an arriving result. Already-committed results replay from their protected history with their original times. No public operation id or admission journal is added.

The protected confirmation controller MUST durably bind each issued occurrence to its admission generation and the approved snapshot, lesson, call and occurrence bindings before issuing confirmation. Preserve that binding and original attestation for the event history's lifetime, independently of public event fields. An event append cannot qualify an occurrence whose issuance binding is unavailable. An unused occurrence issued in an earlier generation is rejected, including after a return to identical content or a forged current-generation label.

After restart an outstanding confirmation is eligible only if its protected issuance binding is recoverable and current; otherwise request fresh confirmation. Missing provenance is unverified; a known mismatch is rejected. Neither settles an objective. Admission and issuance evidence contain no answer, answer hash or conversation. These generation duties are proposed record policy, not an approval of a receipt format, signing scheme or host.

#### Internal replay handoff <a id="records--replay-interface"></a>

The schema theme's internal test interface is:

```python
replay(events: Sequence[Mapping], snapshot_roots: Mapping[str, Path], *,
       receipt_status: Callable[[int, Mapping, int], str]) -> Mapping
```

It returns the whole projection or raises `ContractError` on a stopped replay. Snapshot roots map identities to retained authored folders; all identity, stable-read and structure checks still apply. For an otherwise applicable learner decision, call `receipt_status(event_index, event, expected_generation)`. The index is zero-based in the supplied sequence; expected generation is replay's derived counter. The callback checks the protected issuance generation and original receipt bindings, never treating a public generation label as proof.

The result is exactly `verified`, `unverified` or `rejected`; any other result raises `ContractError`. Missing authority supplies unverified. Known mismatches are rejected. A line already labelled unverified cannot be promoted, even by a verified callback result. Replay checks the original durable attestation without redeeming the occurrence again; identical inputs must reproduce identical outcomes. Loss of previously required verifier authority stops authoritative resume. An untrusted imported view remains unverified and cannot replace authoritative history.

A controlled callback represents an explicitly assumed synthetic authority. It proves neither human provenance nor host qualification. Schemas check event generation shape and record history shape; replay checks equality, derivation and verified sources. The existing `course`, `lesson`, `checks` and `rubric` schemas cover retained authored files. Their structural validity alone does not establish cross-file validity or evidence provenance.

#### Completing a lesson <a id="records--completing"></a>

A `lesson-completed` line applies only when the lesson is not already `passed` and it really passed. A lesson passes when every required check it lists has a latest result of `pass`. A lesson with no required checks, such as an `understand` lesson, passes this check predicate at once. Optional checks do not gate passing. The final lesson also needs its latest applied `explain-judged` to reach the rubric's pass mark: an earlier attempt that met the bar does not count after a later one that did not, and a later one that meets it replaces an earlier miss. A required check that was `not-run` or `error`, or a latest attempt below the bar, means the lesson did not pass, and the completion line is not applied. A runner without a capability a lesson needs cannot record that lesson as passed. It can still teach it, and the learner moving on is recorded as skipped.

The first applied completion sets the status to `passed` and `at` to that line's time. Every later `lesson-completed` for the same lesson is not applied, so a repeated completion neither counts twice nor moves the first time.

#### Settling objectives <a id="records--settling"></a>

Settlement is derived at the moment a completion applies, and it is never an event, so no runner can assert it. Each objective the lesson declares settles when:

1. its own evidence passed: the named check's latest result is pass with its declared evidence kind, even when optional; or the latest explain-back meets the rubric; and
2. every call the lesson declares has state `decided` with `by: learner` and `provenance: verified`; and
3. the lesson was not skipped, which is the case because it is being completed.

A settled objective is added to `objectives` with `at` the completion's time and `snapshot` the current snapshot. One completion can settle several objectives at once. They are added in the order the lesson file declares them, not by name or by the order of their evidence, and a later completion's objectives follow every earlier one. A lesson that declares no calls has nothing to satisfy in the second condition, and zero declared calls is not a missing call. A declared call that is open, absent, decided by the tutor or unverified leaves every objective of that lesson unsettled, while the lesson still passes. A receipt alone never settles anything, because all three conditions are needed, as [the execution contract](/specification/#execution--settlement) says. A settled objective is never discarded: an upgrade moves it unchanged into history. Idempotence and declaration ordering apply separately within each active generation. A later failing check does not unsettle it, because it records what held at completion.

#### Skipping <a id="records--skipping-record"></a>

A `lesson-skipped` line applies only when the lesson is not `passed` and not already `skipped`. It sets the status to `skipped` and `at` to its time. A skipped lesson settles nothing, and it is never shown as passed. A learner who returns to it starts it again, and it then follows the ordinary rules, including passing later. Moving on from a lesson that has passed is not a skip, so a `lesson-skipped` line for a passed lesson is not applied.

### Writing and recovery <a id="records--recovery"></a>

#### Appending <a id="records--appending"></a>

The protected runner serialises writers per course and for the shared course lock. It checks operation admissions under that same lock. A runner appends each event as one write of the JSON text and its LF to `events.jsonl`, then waits until the operating system confirms the bytes are stored before it acts on the event. If the file does not end with LF, the runner first writes LF so a valid unterminated final line is completed and never merged with the next one. The runner appends before it replaces the record, so a crash between the two leaves history ahead of the record, which replay repairs.

#### A partial append and an invalid line <a id="records--damage"></a>

Two different faults can appear in a log. They are told apart so a crash is not mistaken for damage.

| | Partial append | Invalid historical line |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What it is | The last line has no LF and is not a valid event: a write was interrupted | A line that has an LF, or is not the last line, and is not a valid event |
| Cause | A crash or a full disk during an append | Corruption, an edit or a runner bug |
| How a reader treats it | It is not an event. The reader reports an interrupted write | It is skipped and reported as damage, and the rest of the log still applies |
| What the runner does with the bytes | Moves them unchanged to the end of `events.partial` in the same folder, then cuts the log back to its last LF | Nothing. The line stays where it is and is never edited or removed |
| Effect on the record | None | The line contributes nothing, so a lost decision stays open and a lost completion stays unrecorded; nothing is guessed |

A final line without an LF that is a valid event counts as an event, and the runner completes it with an LF before the next append. A reader never repairs history by guessing a missing event. Silently discarding history is forbidden: partial bytes are set aside, never dropped, and damage is always reported. A runner reports an interrupted write once and damage at every resume.

#### Replacing the record <a id="records--atomic-record"></a>

The runner writes the whole new content to a temporary file in the same folder, waits until it is stored, renames it over `record.json` in one step, then confirms the folder entry is stored. It never edits `record.json` in place, so a reader sees either the whole old file or the whole new one. A leftover temporary file is ignored and may be deleted. The lock is replaced the same way.

#### Resuming <a id="records--resume-record"></a>

When a learner returns, the runner first verifies complete protected history and replays committed transitions from retained sources. If a committed transition left install, lock or record behind, [transition recovery](/specification/#records--transition-durability) completes it. The runner then verifies the installed course against the repaired lock by [pinning duty two](/specification/#identity--pinning), and compares replay with `record.json`. If the file is missing, damaged or different, the replay result replaces it, and the learner is told it was rebuilt. The events win over the record.

- A `record.json` with no `events.jsonl` is not evidence of an empty history. Preserve it as `record.orphaned.json` and stop authoritative resume if committed history is missing. Never reset generation from that loss. A genuinely new course with no prior commitment has empty history; an untrusted imported view cannot authorise new progress.
- With genuinely empty history the result is the initial record, which `record.json` need not exist for: `course`, `version`, `snapshot`, `project` and `position` are `null`, `generation` is zero, `lessons` is empty, and every list, including `history`, is empty.
- The runner resumes at the position's lesson and step. If that lesson is `passed` or `skipped`, it resumes at the first lesson after it, in lesson order, that is neither; with none left, the course has ended. A call that is `open` is put again, and the runner never infers an answer, as [the execution contract](/specification/#execution--resuming) says. A help request after resuming is a `hint-given` line and never an attempt.

### Upgrades and changed content <a id="records--upgrades"></a>

An unrequested content change is refused without an event. An explicit upgrade is a committed transition only after the target, prior history and project compatibility have been verified. The learner's editable workspace is never replaced. Same-current and refused requests change no install, lock, record or admission.

#### Durable transition and recovery <a id="records--transition-durability"></a>

1. Under the protected writer boundary, verify old history and the complete target. Require unchanged course name and that the selected project option remains offered. Refuse a change of project binding; a retained local source may change while the editable copy remains untouched.
2. Prepare a complete sibling temporary snapshot, preserving included bytes, links and empty directories. Validate it, durably store every file and directory entry, then atomically publish its retained directory. Verify an existing final folder before reuse; never overwrite a conflicting folder or treat a temporary folder as published.
3. Obtain the learner's explicit instruction and durably append the complete start/upgrade event before changing active install, lock or projection. This append is the commit point. It advances the derived generation and invalidates old admissions; result appends cannot bypass that admission check.
4. Materialise the installed copy from the verified retained target. Atomically replace this course's lock entry using transition source/commit and verified metadata, preserving every other entry. Then atomically replace the complete record. Confirm directory writes and teach only when install, lock and last committed transition agree.

A crash before commitment leaves old state authoritative; an orphan published target is unused. A crash after commitment completes the committed target on recovery, without another event, generation increment or history entry. It never overwrites the learner's workspace or changes historical timestamps. Replay derives the counter exactly once, regardless of which replacements finished. Restart also applies the admission and confirmation rules above.

If required bytes, authority or a trustworthy lock cannot be recovered, stop visibly and preserve damaged material. Never publish a partial record, infer a missing transition from a newer lock or silently roll back. An invalid historical line is still reported; evidence of lost committed history additionally blocks authoritative resume. Complete-tail and partial-append handling remains as [damage](/specification/#records--damage) specifies. Atomic replacement states the required durability contract; synthetic cut-point tests do not prove any real filesystem meets it.

#### Progress after upgrade <a id="records--upgrade-projection"></a>

Each applied upgrade appends exactly the previous `{version, snapshot, generation, position, lessons, calls, objectives}` to `history`. It then changes version/snapshot, increments generation, clears lessons/calls/objectives and sets position to null. Keep project, consent and declined-hook records unchanged. Preserve every event and archived outcome, including skipped and unfinished work. Historical achievements keep their original evidence, identity, order and first completion time.

Only the fresh active segment can complete lessons or settle objectives under the new content. Every new check, explain-back and call needs new evidence. Previously passed lessons receive no automatic credit; matching ids imply no equivalence. The first new lesson waits for its normal learner gate. Null position neither starts it nor creates an attempt. Used occurrences remain used; unused ones keep their original issuance generation.

Returning from old to new to old creates generations one, two and three with two ordered history entries. It never restores generation-one admissions. Asking for the already-current identity is a no-op: no transition, history entry or reset, and otherwise valid current admissions survive. An unavailable, invalid or incompatible target is likewise refused before commitment, without changing active files or workspace. A broken target named by a committed transition instead stops recovery.

Changed `run` strings MUST be disclosed again under the [execution contract](/specification/#execution--command-display). Old check results never substitute for a new run. Required checks gate passing; each objective still needs its own passing evidence, including an optional check it names. Zero declared calls, latest explain-back, declaration-order settlement and completion idempotence retain their meanings within each active segment.

### Privacy <a id="records--privacy"></a>

A runner MUST NOT put any of these in any field of the lock, the record, operation/issuance evidence or an event, whether the field is defined here or not: an answer to a call, text of a conversation, project code or file contents, a hash or other digest of any of those, a credential, a learner identifier, or a machine identifier. The only digests allowed are course identities, which hash course files, and a receipt's `occurrence` is random, not a digest. A hash of a short private answer is easy to guess, so it is no substitute for leaving the answer out.

The records still hold timing and the learner's choices of lesson and project, and receipt identifiers can correlate activity. They stay local, are excluded from Git history, and are never sent anywhere by this contract. A host may keep prompt histories of its own, which no runner can remove. Feedback hooks send their own closed payload, as [the consent contract](/specification/#consent--payload-fields) defines, and never a record.

### Schemas, names and status <a id="records--status"></a>

#### Schema addresses <a id="records--schema-addresses"></a>

The candidate defines eight machine-readable types for later publication: `course`, `lesson`, `checks`, `feedback`, `lock`, `record`, `event` and `rubric`. The lock, the record and one event line are described by the `lock`, `record` and `event` schemas. One `event` schema covers every kind, and `kind` selects the rules. It covers transition source/commit and lesson snapshot/generation; `record` covers derived generation and history. No generation field belongs to a transition or lock. Retained folders use existing authored-file schemas, not an additional replay schema.

**Proposed:** each type has the address `https://agentcourses.io/schemas/<candidate>/<type>.schema.json`, where `<candidate>` is the candidate label. The first candidate label is `1.0.0-rc.1`. The owner confirms the label before any address is published. An address, once published, never changes its bytes, and changed bytes need a new candidate label and a new address. No address is live yet, and no text may say one is until it has been published and checked.

#### Names <a id="records--names"></a>

The contract names only what a reader needs to follow its rules.

| Thing | Name | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The specification | Agent Courses | Named here |
| Its version | `1.0`, in `spec` fields; the candidate label is `1.0.0-rc.1` | Named here |
| The home and schema host | `agentcourses.io` | Decided by the owner |
| The repository | `agentcourses/agentcourses` | Existing |
| Files and folders | `COURSE.md`, `.agents/courses/`, `.agents/courses.lock`, `.agents/records/<course>/`, `.agents/course-snapshots/` | Named here and in [the format contract](/specification/#format--folder-layout) |
| A command, library or package | None | Not part of this contract; chosen at release, after a check that the names are free ⚠️ unverified |

#### Candidate and stable <a id="records--candidate-and-stable"></a>

**Proposed:** this specification is a *candidate* when the owner accepts the published snapshot and its manifest after independent review. The candidate bar is that no contract question is unassigned, the rules have cases, the internal checks pass, and the text makes no claim about hosts, packages or stability. Accepting it qualifies no host and no runner.

A *stable* 1.0.0 is a later step. It belongs to a launch qualification that follows this candidate: a host whose confirmation boundary is qualified, a beginner pilot with real learners and published packages. This candidate sets no pass mark for that step. Until it is met, no runner, course or document may claim stability or conformance.

### Worked traces <a id="records--traces"></a>

Each trace gives a log and the record a reader derives from it. They use one *trace course*: the extended baseline from [the format contract](/specification/#format--baseline-course), where `make.md` also declares the objective `agent-runs-ok` by the check `agent-runs`. The rubric is `pass: 2 of 3`.

| Lesson | Stage | Checks | Calls | Objectives |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `intro` | understand | none | `choose-tool` | none |
| `make` | build | `agent-runs` (observe) | none | `agent-runs-ok` by `agent-runs` |
| `finish` | demonstrate | `final-check` (command) | `choose-check` | `agent-passes` by `final-check`, `agent-explained` by `explain-back` |

In a trace table each line is a JSON object with `at` and `kind` first, written `2026-10-21T<time>Z`. Where a field's value is long, a name stands for it: `S1` and `S2` are the verified identities of the complete trace trees defined below, `O1` is `q7Zk2mR9xT4vB1nC8dLw5A`, and `O2` is `Hs3Fp0yU6eJg2KtN7oVb1Q`. A receipt is `{occurrence, verifier: example-verifier}`. Every lesson-progress line explicitly expands to `snapshot=S1 generation=1` unless stated otherwise. Every transition expands to `source=local` and has no `commit` or `generation`; these defaults are part of the complete input, not permission to omit required fields. Every other field is written `name=value`. Verified examples assume a protected synthetic issuance binding for the stated generation and original receipt; they qualify no host.

#### Complete trace inputs <a id="records--trace-inputs"></a>

The complete authored tree below fixes every input byte for the worked traces. Each fenced file ends in one LF. It is the accepted extended format baseline with a declared make objective and concrete check fixtures; it is not the identity-vector toy tree. The old identity is `sha256:c35480cb14f89d9e89719faa2452a5b494d58ba2f80cb3fe26abd2d0346f4ca4`, and the new identity is `sha256:727fef18c5013d7ce206e0f8253218857e433f673af14129278e6bb097b927a6`.

`S1` names this tree. `S2` is a copy with exactly two replacements: `COURSE.md` version `"1.0.0"` becomes `"1.1.0"`, and the check command in `checks/checks.yaml` changes from `python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py"` to `python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py" --strict`. The check implementation still accepts that argument; the changed command must nevertheless be disclosed. All other bytes remain identical. Variant trees state their precise edit and must use their own recomputed identities, never S1 or S2's folder labels.

<details>
<summary>Exact authored files for sample-course</summary>

`COURSE.md`:

```markdown
---
name: sample-course
description: Build a one-file agent and explain it. For beginners.
version: "1.0.0"
license: Apache-2.0
---

# Sample course

You'll build a small agent.

## Outcome

`agent.py`: an agent that passes `final-check`.

## Lessons

1. [Meet the agent](lessons/intro.md)
2. [Make the agent](lessons/make.md)
3. [Finish and explain](lessons/finish.md)
```

`checks/checks.yaml`:

```yaml
- id: final-check
  run: python "$AGENT_COURSE_DIR/checks/final_check.py"
  verify: agent.py defines an agent
  timeout: 30
  network: false
- id: agent-runs
  kind: observe
  verify: agent.py exists and defines an agent
```

`checks/final_check.py`:

```python
from pathlib import Path
raise SystemExit(0 if "agent" in Path("agent.py").read_text() else 1)
```

`checks/fixtures/final-check/fail/agent.py`:

```python
value = None
```

`checks/fixtures/final-check/pass/agent.py`:

```python
agent = object()
```

`lessons/finish.md`:

```markdown
---
title: Finish and explain
stage: demonstrate
checks: [final-check]
calls:
  - id: choose-check
    ask: Which behaviour will the final check protect?
objectives:
  - id: agent-passes
    text: The agent passes the final check
    by: final-check
  - id: agent-explained
    text: The learner explains the agent
    by: explain-back
---

## Goal

You can show the agent works and say why.

## Explain back

Explain which tool your agent calls and why you chose it.
```

`lessons/intro.md`:

```markdown
---
title: Meet the agent
stage: understand
calls:
  - id: choose-tool
    ask: Which tool will your agent call first?
---

## Goal

You can say what an agent tool is.
```

`lessons/make.md`:

```markdown
---
title: Make the agent
stage: build
checks: [agent-runs]
objectives:
  - id: agent-runs-ok
    text: The agent runs
    by: agent-runs
---

## Goal

Your agent runs.

## Build

Ask the learner how to start the agent, then create `agent.py` with them.
```

`tutor/finish.rubric.md`:

```markdown
---
pass: 2 of 3
---
- **Names the output type** and says why a typed result is safer than prose
- **Describes the tool call**: what triggers it and what it returns
- **Names one failure mode** and how the checks would catch it
```

| 2 | 09:00:05 | `project-chosen` project=&#123;kind: own, path: .&#125; |
| 3 | 09:01:00 | `lesson-started` lesson=intro |
| 4 | 09:01:05 | `step-entered` lesson=intro step=goal |
| 5 | 09:02:00 | `call-put` lesson=intro call=choose-tool |
| 6 | 09:03:00 | `call-decided` lesson=intro call=choose-tool by=learner provenance=verified receipt=&#123;O1&#125; snapshot=S1 |
| 7 | 09:04:00 | `lesson-completed` lesson=intro |
| 8 | 09:05:00 | `lesson-started` lesson=make |
| 9 | 09:06:00 | `check-run` lesson=make check=agent-runs result=fail evidence=observed |
| 10 | 09:08:00 | `check-run` lesson=make check=agent-runs result=pass evidence=observed |
| 11 | 09:09:00 | `lesson-completed` lesson=make |

The record after line 11, where `<S1>` stands for `S1`:

```json
{
  "spec": "1.0",
  "course": "sample-course",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "snapshot": "<S1>",
  "generation": 1,
  "history": [],
  "project": { "kind": "own", "path": "." },
  "position": { "lesson": "make", "step": "goal" },
  "lessons": {
    "intro": { "status": "passed", "at": "2026-10-21T09:04:00Z" },
    "make": {
      "status": "passed",
      "checks": { "agent-runs": { "last": "pass", "runs": 2 } },
      "at": "2026-10-21T09:09:00Z"
    }
  },
  "calls": [
    { "id": "choose-tool", "lesson": "intro", "state": "decided", "by": "learner", "provenance": "verified" }
  ],
  "objectives": [
    { "id": "agent-runs-ok", "lesson": "make", "evidence": "observed", "by": "agent-runs",
      "at": "2026-10-21T09:09:00Z", "snapshot": "<S1>" }
  ],
  "consent": [],
  "declined": []

```

Line 1 sets the course and snapshot. Line 2 sets the project. Lines 3 to 6 start `intro` and record its call as decided by the learner and verified, and line 7 passes it because it lists no checks. Lines 8 to 11 run the check twice, so `runs` is 2 and `last` is `pass`, and pass `make`. `make` declares no calls, so its objective settles at line 11. The position is the latest `lesson-started`, which is `make` at step `goal`. The time of the passed lessons is the completion line's time.

#### The finishing lesson <a id="records--trace-finish"></a>

These lines follow the opening. Each variant changes line 14 only, except *Never put*, which also removes line 13.

| Line | Time | Event |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 12 | 09:10:00 | `lesson-started` lesson=finish |
| 13 | 09:11:00 | `call-put` lesson=finish call=choose-check |
| 14 | 09:12:00 | the call line, which each variant below sets |
| 15 | 09:15:00 | `check-run` lesson=finish check=final-check result=pass evidence=checked |
| 16 | 09:16:00 | `step-entered` lesson=finish step=explain-back |
| 17 | 09:18:00 | `explain-judged` lesson=finish met=2 of=3 |
| 18 | 09:19:00 | `lesson-completed` lesson=finish |

The base result for the finishing lesson is the opening record with these changes: `position` is `finish` at `explain-back`; `lessons.finish` is `{status: passed, checks: {final-check: {last: pass, runs: 1}}, explain: {met: 2, of: 3, passed: true}, at: 2026-10-21T09:19:00Z}`; and a call entry for `choose-check` is added. The objectives change in each variant.

| Variant of line 14 | `choose-check` entry | Objectives after line 18 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="records--trace-learner-verified"></a>**Learner, verified.** `call-decided` lesson=finish call=choose-check by=learner provenance=verified receipt=&#123;O2&#125; snapshot=S1 | decided, learner, verified | `agent-runs-ok`, `agent-passes` and `agent-explained`, the last two at 09:19:00 with evidence `checked` and `explained` |
| <a id="records--trace-tutor-call"></a>**Tutor decided.** `call-decided` lesson=finish call=choose-check by=tutor snapshot=S1 | decided, tutor | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-unverified-call"></a>**Learner, unverified.** `call-decided` lesson=finish call=choose-check by=learner provenance=unverified snapshot=S1 | decided, learner, unverified | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-open-call"></a>**Put, never answered.** Line 14 is absent | open | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-missing-call"></a>**Never put.** Lines 13 and 14 are both absent | no entry | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-replayed-receipt"></a>**Repeated receipt.** `call-decided` lesson=finish call=choose-check by=learner provenance=verified receipt=&#123;O1&#125; snapshot=S1 | open: line 14 is not applied because `O1` was used at line 6 | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-binding-snapshot"></a>**Wrong snapshot.** The learner decision with receipt=&#123;O2&#125; and snapshot=S2 | open: line 14 is not applied because it names another snapshot | `agent-runs-ok` only |
| <a id="records--trace-binding-lesson"></a>**Wrong lesson.** The learner decision with receipt=&#123;O2&#125; but lesson=make | open: line 14 is not applied because `make` does not declare `choose-check` | `agent-runs-ok` only |

In *Learner, verified*, `agent-passes` is added before `agent-explained`, the order `finish.md` declares them. Name order would put `agent-explained` first, and neither the order of the evidence nor name order is used.

In every variant the lesson `finish` passes, because its check passed and the explain-back met 2 of 3. Calls decide only settlement. In the last three variants the line is reported as not applied, and nothing in the record shows it. If a call is missing, `agent-passes` and `agent-explained` stay unsettled, and `agent-runs-ok` stays settled because `make` declares no calls.

History contains the opening segment at generation 1, including `agent-runs-ok` with its original `S1` and 09:09:00 time. Project and consent remain unchanged |
| <a id="records--trace-changed-content"></a>**Changed content** | The opening log. The installed folder was edited, so its identity is not `S1`; the lock still says `S1` | No line is written and the record is unchanged. The runner refuses to teach and tells the learner |
| <a id="records--trace-consent"></a>**Consent** | Lines 1 and 2, then 03 `consent-changed` destination=feedback.example.com state=granted, 04 the same destination state=withdrawn, 05 `consent-changed` destination=other.example.net state=declined hook=rating lesson=make | `consent` is one entry, `{destination: feedback.example.com, state: withdrawn}`. `declined` is one entry, `{destination: other.example.net, hook: rating, lesson: make}` |
| <a id="records--trace-not-started"></a>**Lines before `lesson-started`** | Lines 1 and 2, then 03 `check-run` make agent-runs pass observed, 04 `lesson-completed` make, 05 `step-entered` make step=build, 06 `explain-judged` finish met=3 of=3, then 07 `lesson-started` make, 08 `check-run` make agent-runs pass observed (09:06:00), 09 `lesson-completed` make (09:07:00) | Lines 3 to 6 are not applied, because no `lesson-started` has applied for their lessons, and they leave no trace. The result has one lesson, `make`, `passed` at 09:07:00 with `agent-runs` last `pass`, runs 1 (not 2), position `make` at `goal`, and `agent-runs-ok` settled |
| <a id="records--trace-explain-latest-miss"></a>**Two attempts, the later one misses** | The opening, then 12 `lesson-started` finish, 13 `call-put` choose-check, 14 the learner-verified `call-decided` with receipt=&#123;O2&#125;, 15 `check-run` final-check pass checked, 16 `step-entered` explain-back, 17 `explain-judged` met=2 of=3 (09:18:00), 18 `explain-judged` met=1 of=3 (09:19:00), 19 `lesson-completed` finish (09:20:00) | Line 19 is not applied, because the latest attempt, 1 of 3, is below the bar of 2. `finish` is `started`, `explain` is `{met: 1, of: 3, passed: false}`, and no objective settles beyond `agent-runs-ok` |
| <a id="records--trace-explain-latest-pass"></a>**Two attempts, the later one passes** | The same lines, with line 17 `met=1 of=3` and line 18 `met=2 of=3` | Line 19 is applied. `finish` is `passed` at 09:20:00 and `explain` is `{met: 2, of: 3, passed: true}`. `objectives` is `agent-runs-ok` (09:09:00), then `agent-passes` and `agent-explained` (both 09:20:00), in the order `finish.md` declares them |
| <a id="records--trace-upgrade-resume"></a>**Resume after an upgrade** | Opening, then `course-upgraded` from=S1 to=S2 version=1.1.0 source=local at 10:00:00. Then `check-run` make agent-runs pass observed snapshot=S1 generation=1 at 10:01:00, and `lesson-started` finish snapshot=S2 generation=2 at 10:02:00. Both retained folders are present | The stale check is not applied before lookup. The active record is generation 2, position finish/goal, lessons `{finish: {status: started}}`, no active calls/objectives. History holds exactly the generation-one opening segment; old make evidence and 09:09:00 time remain unchanged |
| <a id="records--trace-missing-replay"></a>**Missing retained source** | Same log, but remove the retained S1 folder | Replay stops at the first commitment, names S1 and its missing source, writes no record/lock, and permits no teaching or settlement. Restore the exact authored folder and verify it before replay; then the preceding trace reproduces |

### Record cases <a id="records--example-records"></a>

These cases use the trace course. Where a case names a trace, its result is the one that trace states.

| Case | Baseline and change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-verified-accepted"></a>Verified decision on a qualified host | A host whose boundary is qualified; the runner writes `by=learner provenance=verified receipt={O2}` and the receipt holds only `occurrence` and `verifier` | Record structure | Accepted | A verified decision carries a receipt of exactly two values |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-unqualified-accepted"></a>Unqualified host | The same call on a host with no qualified boundary; the runner writes `provenance=unverified` and no receipt | Record structure | Accepted | Without a shown boundary every learner decision is unverified |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-unqualified-verified-rejected"></a>Unqualified host claims verified | The same host; the runner writes `provenance=verified` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A runner MUST write `unverified` unless the boundary is qualified |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-answer-rejected"></a>Receipt holds the answer | The receipt has a third value holding the learner's typed answer | Runner behaviour | Rejected | No answer text may enter a receipt or any field |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-answer-hash-rejected"></a>Occurrence is an answer hash | The `occurrence` is the SHA-256 of the typed answer | Runner behaviour | Rejected | An occurrence is random and never derived from anything the learner typed |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-unverified-with-receipt-rejected"></a>Unverified with a receipt | `by=learner provenance=unverified` with a receipt | Record structure | Rejected | Only a verified decision carries a receipt |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-verified-without-rejected"></a>Verified without a receipt | `by=learner provenance=verified` and no receipt | Record structure | Rejected | A verified decision MUST carry a receipt |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-tutor-provenance-rejected"></a>Tutor decision with provenance | `by=tutor provenance=verified` | Record structure | Rejected | A tutor decision carries no provenance and no receipt |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-short-occurrence-rejected"></a>Short occurrence | A receipt whose `occurrence` has 8 characters | Record structure | Rejected | An occurrence has 22 to 128 characters |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-repeated-rejected"></a>Receipt used twice | See [Repeated receipt](/specification/#records--trace-replayed-receipt) | Record replay | Rejected | A seen `occurrence` makes the later line not applied |
| <a id="records--case-receipt-binding-rejected"></a>Receipt bound elsewhere | See [Wrong snapshot](/specification/#records--trace-binding-snapshot) and [Wrong lesson](/specification/#records--trace-binding-lesson) | Record replay | Rejected | A decision for another snapshot or lesson is not applied |
| <a id="records--case-files-placement-accepted"></a>Records excluded, lock shared | A Git workspace; the runner lists `.agents/records/` in `.git/info/exclude` and leaves the lock committed | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Records are personal and the lock is shared |
| <a id="records--case-files-gitignore-rejected"></a>Records ignored for everyone | The runner adds `.agents/records/` to the shared `.gitignore` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Exclusion uses `.git/info/exclude`, never the shared file |
| <a id="records--case-files-lock-excluded-rejected"></a>Lock excluded | The runner lists `.agents/courses.lock` in `.git/info/exclude` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The lock MUST NOT be excluded |
| <a id="records--case-line-valid-accepted"></a>A valid line | `{"at":"2026-10-21T09:00:00Z","kind":"lesson-started","lesson":"intro","snapshot":"sha256:aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa","generation":1}` followed by LF | Record structure | Accepted | One object, a time, a kind and the kind's fields |
| <a id="records--case-line-time-rejected"></a>Time with a fraction | The same line with `"at":"2026-10-21T09:00:00.5Z"` | Record structure | Rejected | A time has no fractions |
| <a id="records--case-line-duplicate-key-rejected"></a>Repeated key | The same line with `"lesson"` written twice | Record structure | Rejected | A line has no repeated key |
| <a id="records--case-line-long-rejected"></a>Line over 4096 bytes | The same line padded with an unknown field to 5000 bytes | Record structure | Rejected | A line is at most 4096 bytes |
| <a id="records--case-line-unknown-kind-accepted"></a>Unknown kind | See [Unknown kind and unknown field](/specification/#records--trace-unknown-kind) | Record replay | Accepted | A reader keeps an unknown kind and it changes nothing |
| <a id="records--case-line-unknown-progress-rejected"></a>Unknown kind creates progress | A line of kind `lesson-finished` makes `make` passed | Record replay | Rejected | An unknown kind never creates progress |
| <a id="records--case-line-unknown-field-copied-rejected"></a>Unknown field copied | The reader copies `extra` into the record | Record replay | Rejected | Unknown fields are ignored and not copied |
| <a id="records--case-followed-ignored-accepted"></a>Followed field ignored | A `call-decided` line with `followed=recommendation` | Record replay | Accepted | The field is not defined, so it is ignored and the call is judged without it |
| <a id="records--case-followed-settles-rejected"></a>Followed field affects settlement | A runner treats `followed=recommendation` as making the call verified | Runner behaviour | Rejected | An unknown field never affects ownership or settlement |
| <a id="records--case-project-own-accepted"></a>Own project | `project={kind: own, path: .}` | Record structure | Accepted | An own project records its base path |
| <a id="records--case-project-git-accepted"></a>Git project | `project={kind: git, path: services/api, source: gh:example-co/payments@v3.4.0#services/api, commit: 9f3c2e1a4b5d6c7e8f90a1b2c3d4e5f607182930}` | Record structure | Accepted | A Git project records its source and resolved commit |
| <a id="records--case-project-local-accepted"></a>Local project | `project={kind: local, path: work/local-app, source: ./examples/app}` | Record structure | Accepted | The copy is recorded and no commit is invented |
| <a id="records--case-project-git-no-commit-rejected"></a>Git project without a commit | The Git project without `commit` | Record structure | Rejected | A Git project needs its resolved commit |
| <a id="records--case-project-own-commit-rejected"></a>Own project with a commit | `project={kind: own, path: ., commit: 9f3c2e1a4b5d6c7e8f90a1b2c3d4e5f607182930}` | Record structure | Rejected | Only a Git project records a commit |
| <a id="records--case-project-local-dot-rejected"></a>Local project at the base | `project={kind: local, path: ., source: ./examples/app}` | Record structure | Rejected | A local destination is a child path, not the base |
| <a id="records--case-project-credential-rejected"></a>Credential in the source | `project={kind: git, ..., source: git+https://user:token@git.example.com/team/repo.git}` | Record structure | Rejected | A source never holds a credential |
| <a id="records--case-lock-git-accepted"></a>Lock entry for a Git source | An entry with `name`, `version`, `source`, `commit` and `identity` | Record structure | Accepted | A Git source records its commit and identity |
| <a id="records--case-lock-local-accepted"></a>Lock entry for a local source | An entry with `source: local`, an `identity` and no `commit` | Record structure | Accepted | A local install stores the word `local`, never a path, and has no commit |
| <a id="records--case-lock-local-absolute-rejected"></a>Absolute home path as the source | An entry with `source: /Users/ada/courses/sample-course` | Record structure | Rejected | A local path is never stored, because it would put a user name into a committed file |
| <a id="records--case-lock-local-relative-rejected"></a>Relative or home-relative path as the source | An entry with `source: ./courses/sample-course`, another with `../courses/sample-course` and another with `~/courses/sample-course` | Record structure | Rejected | The only local `source` is `local`; any path is refused |
| <a id="records--case-lock-local-reinstall-accepted"></a>Local reinstall | A learner reinstalls a local folder: the lock still says `local`, the learner names the folder again, and its identity is compared with the entry | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The lock needs no path, and identity still decides whether the folder is the pinned course |
| <a id="records--case-lock-duplicate-rejected"></a>Two entries for one course | Two entries named `sample-course` | Record structure | Rejected | Names are unique in the lock |
| <a id="records--case-lock-identity-rejected"></a>Short identity | An entry whose `identity` is `sha256:1234` | Record structure | Rejected | An identity is `sha256:` and 64 hexadecimal digits |
| <a id="records--case-record-counts-accepted"></a>No stored counts | The opening record, which holds statuses only | Record structure | Accepted | Counts and percentages are derived, never stored |
| <a id="records--case-record-counts-rejected"></a>Counts stored | The record also holds `"lessons_passed": 2` and `"percent": 66` | Record structure | Rejected | Counts and percentages are never stored |
| <a id="records--case-record-learner-id-rejected"></a>Learner identifier stored | The record holds an opaque `learner` value | Record structure | Rejected | The record holds no learner identifier |
| <a id="records--case-record-complete-accepted"></a>Complete after the final lesson | The base finishing result, with `finish` passed | Record replay | Accepted | A course is complete when its final lesson has passed |
| <a id="records--case-record-complete-rejected"></a>Complete with the final lesson skipped | The opening with `finish` skipped | Record replay | Rejected | A skipped final lesson does not complete the course |
| <a id="records--case-settle-all-accepted"></a>Settled with everything verified | See [Learner, verified](/specification/#records--trace-learner-verified) | Record replay | Accepted | Passed evidence, verified learner calls and no skip settle every objective |
| <a id="records--case-settle-tutor-rejected"></a>Tutor call settles | See [Tutor decided](/specification/#records--trace-tutor-call) | Record replay | Rejected | A tutor decision leaves the lesson's objectives unsettled |
| <a id="records--case-settle-unverified-rejected"></a>Unverified call settles | See [Learner, unverified](/specification/#records--trace-unverified-call) | Record replay | Rejected | An unverified decision leaves them unsettled |
| <a id="records--case-settle-open-rejected"></a>Open call settles | See [Put, never answered](/specification/#records--trace-open-call) | Record replay | Rejected | A call put but not answered leaves them unsettled |
| <a id="records--case-settle-missing-rejected"></a>Missing call settles | See [Never put](/specification/#records--trace-missing-call) | Record replay | Rejected | A call never put leaves them unsettled |
| <a id="records--case-settle-zero-calls-accepted"></a>Zero declared calls | The opening record, where `make` declares no calls | Record replay | Accepted | A lesson that declares no calls has nothing to satisfy, so its objective settles |
| <a id="records--case-settle-event-rejected"></a>Settlement written as an event | A line of kind `objective-settled` for `agent-passes` | Record replay | Rejected | Settlement is derived; no event creates it |
| <a id="records--case-settle-later-failure-accepted"></a>A later failure | After the learner-verified trace, a later `check-run` of `final-check` fails | Record replay | Accepted | The settled objectives stay, because they record what held at completion |
| <a id="records--case-complete-repeat-rejected"></a>Repeated completion | See [Repeated completion](/specification/#records--trace-repeated-completion) | Record replay | Rejected | A later completion is not applied and the first time stays |
| <a id="records--case-complete-unproven-rejected"></a>Completion without evidence | See [Completion without evidence](/specification/#records--trace-no-evidence) | Record replay | Rejected | A lesson passes only when its checks passed |
| <a id="records--case-complete-capability-accepted"></a>Check could not run | See [Check not run](/specification/#records--trace-not-run) | Record replay | Accepted | The lesson is recorded as skipped, never as passed |
| <a id="records--case-skip-recorded-accepted"></a>Skip recorded as skipped | See [Skip](/specification/#records--trace-skip) | Record replay | Accepted | A skip remains a skip and settles nothing |
| <a id="records--case-skip-passed-rejected"></a>Skip recorded as passed | Line 10 of the skip trace is `lesson-completed` and a reader treats the lesson as passed | Record replay | Rejected | A completion without passing evidence is not applied |
| <a id="records--case-skip-return-accepted"></a>Skipped lesson passed later | See [Skip, then pass](/specification/#records--trace-skip-return) | Record replay | Accepted | A returning learner passes by the ordinary rules |
| <a id="records--case-skip-passed-lesson-rejected"></a>Skipping a passed lesson | See [Skip a passed lesson](/specification/#records--trace-skip-after-pass) | Record replay | Rejected | Moving on from a passed lesson is not a skip |
| <a id="records--case-help-not-attempt-accepted"></a>Hints are not attempts | See [Help is not an attempt](/specification/#records--trace-hints) | Record replay | Accepted | Help never adds to `runs` |
| <a id="records--case-empty-initial-accepted"></a>Empty history | See [Empty history](/specification/#records--trace-empty) | Record replay | Accepted | An empty log gives the initial record |
| <a id="records--case-empty-invented-rejected"></a>Invented progress from nothing | A runner answers an empty log with a first lesson `started` and a settled objective | Record replay | Rejected | An empty history has no attempt, completion or objective |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-partial-accepted"></a>Interrupted write | See [Interrupted write](/specification/#records--trace-interrupted-write) | Record replay | Accepted with a warning | The fragment is set aside, never dropped, and the interrupted write is reported |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-partial-dropped-rejected"></a>Fragment discarded | The runner cuts the log back to line 11 and deletes the fragment | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Silently discarding history is forbidden |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-tail-accepted"></a>Valid tail without LF | See [Valid tail without LF](/specification/#records--trace-complete-tail) | Record replay | Accepted | A complete event counts and the runner completes its line |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-invalid-rejected"></a>Invalid historical line | See [Invalid historical line](/specification/#records--trace-invalid-line) | Record replay | Rejected | The line is skipped, kept in the file and reported |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-invalid-guessed-rejected"></a>Lost decision guessed | A reader rebuilds the lost decision as `by=learner provenance=verified` from the project files | Record replay | Rejected | A reader never repairs history by guessing |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-invalid-edited-rejected"></a>Damaged line deleted | The runner removes the invalid line from the file | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A line in the log is never edited or removed |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-rebuild-accepted"></a>Damaged record rebuilt | See [Damaged record](/specification/#records--trace-rebuild) | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The events win over the record |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-record-trusted-rejected"></a>Damaged record trusted | The runner resumes from the `record.json` that says `finish` passed | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A record that disagrees with the log is replaced |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-orphan-accepted"></a>Record without a log | A record at generation 1 but no events; preserve it as `record.orphaned.json` and stop authoritative resume | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Missing committed history cannot authorise generation zero or new progress |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-atomic-accepted"></a>Atomic replacement | The runner writes a temporary file, stores it and renames it over `record.json` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | A reader sees the whole old file or the whole new one |
| <a id="records--case-recovery-inplace-rejected"></a>Edited in place | The runner rewrites `record.json` in place and crashes half way | Runner behaviour | Rejected | The runner replaces the whole file, never edits it in place |
| <a id="records--case-resume-open-call-accepted"></a>Open call put again | The opening, then a log that ends with `call-put` for `choose-check` and no answer; the runner puts it again and writes a new `call-put` | Runner behaviour | Accepted | An open call is put again and never inferred |
| <a id="records--case-resume-passed-lesson-accepted"></a>Resume after a passed lesson | The opening record, whose position lesson `make` has passed | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The runner resumes at `finish`, the first lesson that is neither passed nor skipped |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-kept-accepted"></a>Authored folders kept per identity | See [Resume after an upgrade](/specification/#records--trace-upgrade-resume) | Record replay | Accepted | Each applied transition loads its verified retained folder, so an upgrade does not change the past |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-installed-rejected"></a>Old lines replayed with the installed course | The same trace, but replay reads the installed `S2` course for lines 1 to 11 | Record replay | Rejected | Replay never reads the installed course, so old `make` evidence is verified against S1 before being archived |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-latest-rejected"></a>Latest snapshot substituted | A runner finds no retained folder for `S1` and replays lines 1 to 11 with the `S2` data | Record replay | Rejected | Replay never substitutes another snapshot |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-missing-accepted"></a>Missing data stops replay | See [Missing replay data](/specification/#records--trace-missing-replay) | Runner behaviour | Accepted | The runner stops, tells the learner and writes nothing |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-missing-overwritten-rejected"></a>Partial result written | The same log; the runner writes the record it could derive from lines 12 onward over `record.json` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A partial replay never replaces the record |
| <a id="records--case-replay-data-learner-rejected"></a>Learner data copied into retained source | The runner copies a learner workspace into the retained authored folder | Record structure | Rejected | Only authored identity-included source is retained |
| <a id="records--case-start-unstarted-rejected"></a>Lines before `lesson-started` | See [Lines before `lesson-started`](/specification/#records--trace-not-started) | Record replay | Rejected | A line naming a lesson with no applied `lesson-started` is not applied |
| <a id="records--case-start-then-run-accepted"></a>Same lines after `lesson-started` | The same `check-run` and `lesson-completed` written after line 07 | Record replay | Accepted | Once the lesson has started, they apply in order |
| <a id="records--case-explain-latest-miss-rejected"></a>Latest attempt misses | See [Two attempts, the later one misses](/specification/#records--trace-explain-latest-miss) | Record replay | Rejected | An earlier passing attempt does not count after a later miss |
| <a id="records--case-explain-latest-pass-accepted"></a>Latest attempt passes | See [Two attempts, the later one passes](/specification/#records--trace-explain-latest-pass) | Record replay | Accepted | A later attempt that meets the bar replaces an earlier miss |
| <a id="records--case-settle-order-accepted"></a>Settlement in declaration order | See [Two attempts, the later one passes](/specification/#records--trace-explain-latest-pass) | Record replay | Accepted | One completion adds `agent-passes` then `agent-explained`, as `finish.md` declares them |
| <a id="records--case-settle-order-name-rejected"></a>Settlement in name order | The same completion adds `agent-explained` first because it sorts first | Record replay | Rejected | The order is declaration order, never name order or evidence order |
| <a id="records--case-upgrade-explicit-accepted"></a>Explicit upgrade | See [Explicit upgrade](/specification/#records--trace-upgrade) | Record replay | Accepted | History is kept and settled objectives keep their snapshot |
| <a id="records--case-upgrade-resettled-rejected"></a>Upgrade re-settles | After the upgrade, the reader moves `agent-runs-ok` to `S2` | Record replay | Rejected | Nothing is re-settled by an upgrade |
| <a id="records--case-upgrade-changed-refused-accepted"></a>Changed content refused | See [Changed content](/specification/#records--trace-changed-content) | Runner behaviour | Accepted | An unrequested change is refused and writes nothing |
| <a id="records--case-consent-stored-accepted"></a>Consent stored | See [Consent](/specification/#records--trace-consent) | Record replay | Accepted | A withdrawal and a skip survive a closed session |
| <a id="records--case-consent-silence-stored-rejected"></a>Silence written as consent | The runner writes `granted` because the learner did not answer | Runner behaviour | Rejected | Silence is a no and writes nothing |
| <a id="records--case-privacy-answer-rejected"></a>Answer in an unknown field | A `call-decided` line carries the typed answer in a field called `answer` | Runner behaviour | Rejected | No field, known or unknown, holds an answer |
| <a id="records--case-privacy-hash-rejected"></a>Hash of an answer | A line carries a SHA-256 of the typed answer | Runner behaviour | Rejected | A hash of free text is never stored |
| <a id="records--case-privacy-conversation-rejected"></a>Conversation text | An event holds the tutor's recommendation text | Runner behaviour | Rejected | No field holds conversation text |
| <a id="records--case-privacy-identifier-rejected"></a>Machine identifier | An event holds the host's machine name | Runner behaviour | Rejected | No field holds a machine identifier |
| <a id="records--case-schema-address-accepted"></a>Schema address | The `record` type in candidate `1.0.0-rc.1` is `https://agentcourses.io/schemas/1.0.0-rc.1/record.schema.json` | Candidate wording | Accepted | The address follows the pattern |
| <a id="records--case-schema-address-reused-rejected"></a>Address reused for new bytes | A published address serves changed bytes | Candidate wording | Rejected | A published address never changes its bytes |
| <a id="records--case-schema-address-live-rejected"></a>Address claimed live early | The text says the schema addresses are live before publication | Candidate wording | Rejected | No address is live until it has been published and checked |
| <a id="records--case-names-accepted"></a>Names | The candidate names the specification, its version, the home and the file paths, and no command or package | Candidate wording | Accepted | Only what the rules need is named |
| <a id="records--case-names-package-rejected"></a>Package name claimed | The candidate names a command and says its package name is free | Candidate wording | Rejected | Availability is a release-time check and unverified |
| <a id="records--case-status-candidate-accepted"></a>Candidate wording | The candidate says it is a candidate, that no host is qualified and that stability is later | Candidate wording | Accepted | Candidate status claims no host, package or stability |
| <a id="records--case-status-stable-rejected"></a>Stable or conformant claimed | The candidate or a runner says it is stable or conforming | Candidate wording | Rejected | No stability or conformance claim is allowed yet |
| <a id="records--case-status-approved-rejected"></a>Fields called owner-approved | The candidate says the owner approved the event and field names | Candidate wording | Rejected | Plan acceptance, a link check or a review is not the owner's approval of a choice |

### Transition and admission cases <a id="records--transition-cases"></a>

These cases use the complete trace inputs and opening above. Every case is independent unless it explicitly names another trace. Start and upgrade events use source local with no commit unless the case changes it. Runner behaviour cases describe protected operations; replay cases describe durable input, so a refused live request never masquerades as a broken commitment.

| Case | Complete baseline and decisive change | Judged as | Outcome | Expected result and normative clause |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="records--case-lookup-current"></a>Current decision applies | Opening plus finishing learner-verified variant; S1 retained and O2 issuance bound to generation 1 | Record replay | Accepted | Finish passes and adds agent-passes then agent-explained at 09:19:00; generation 1 and empty history remain. [Rule](/specification/#records--applying-events); [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-replay) |
| <a id="records--case-lookup-unknown"></a>Wrong snapshot never loads its folder | Change only line 14 snapshot to an otherwise unknown valid identity; its retained folder is absent | Record replay | Accepted | Line 14 is not applied before lookup; finish passes but only agent-runs-ok remains settled. [Rule](/specification/#records--applying-events); [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-replay) |

| <a id="records--case-confirmation-old"></a>Confirmation recovery old | Current-generation open call after restart; Recover a generation-one binding after revisit at generation 3 | Record replay | Accepted | Known mismatch is rejected; event label cannot repair it. | <a id="records--case-confirmation-authority"></a>Confirmation recovery authority | Current-generation open call after restart; Lose authority required to replay previously established history | Runner behaviour | Accepted | Stop authoritative resume; never rewrite established history as newly verified. | <a id="records--case-callback-bad"></a>Invalid callback result | Otherwise applicable learner decision; callback returns yes instead of one of the three statuses | Record replay | Rejected | Raise ContractError; no partial projection. [Rule](/specification/#records--replay-interface); [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-replay-interface) |

#### Projection and schema boundary cases <a id="records--projection-cases"></a>

These cases mutate a complete derived projection or transition, one field at a time. A shape failure never supplies missing authority. Reconstruction uses the same protected history and required sources; it is unavailable when those are lost.

| Case | Baseline and decisive change | Judged as | Outcome | Deciding rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="records--case-projection-current"></a>Complete projection | The opening record includes generation 1 and history [] | Record structure | Accepted | [Record fields](/specification/#records--record-fields) require both; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-generation-missing"></a>No record generation | Remove generation from the opening record | Record structure | Rejected | Rebuild; never trust an absent counter; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-generation-wrong"></a>False derived counter | Opening record says generation 3 although its log has only one applied start | Record replay | Rejected | Replay derives 1 and replaces stale projection; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-generation) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-missing"></a>No history list | Remove history from the opening record | Record structure | Rejected | Rebuild the required empty list; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-null"></a>Null archive identity | Explicit-upgrade record history[0].snapshot is null | Record structure | Rejected | Archived identity/version are non-null; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-zero"></a>Zero archived generation | Explicit-upgrade history[0].generation is 0 | Record structure | Rejected | An archive is a previously active positive generation; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-generation) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-nested"></a>Recursive history | Add history: [] inside explicit-upgrade history[0] | Record structure | Rejected | Archive entries have exactly seven fields and no nested history; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-project"></a>Project copied into history | Add project to explicit-upgrade history[0] | Record structure | Rejected | Project and consent remain only in the active root; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-fields) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-restamped"></a>Old achievement restamped | Explicit-upgrade history[0].objectives[0].at becomes 10:00:00 instead of 09:09:00 | Record replay | Rejected | Replay restores its original completion time and S1 evidence; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-upgrade) |
| <a id="records--case-projection-history-reordered"></a>History reversed | Revisit record has generation-two entry before generation-one entry | Record replay | Rejected | Replay restores transition order 1,2; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-upgrade) |
| <a id="records--case-lock-generation"></a>Counter in the lock | Add generation: 1 to an otherwise valid course lock entry | Record structure | Rejected | The lock never carries generation; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-lock) |
| <a id="records--case-claim-host-qualified"></a>Synthetic callback claimed to qualify a host | A report labels an actual host qualified solely because replay used a controlled verified callback | Candidate wording | Rejected | [Replay handoff](/specification/#records--replay-interface) proves no actual host; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-replay-interface) |
| <a id="records--case-claim-durability"></a>Crash oracle claimed as filesystem proof | A report treats a simulated cut-point oracle as proof of real durable writes | Candidate wording | Rejected | Real persistence qualification remains separate; [requirement](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/requirements.md#req-record-transition-durability) |

Source: [spec/records.md](https://github.com/agentcourses/agentcourses/blob/main/spec/records.md).
